sources: [B live_verified] src-worldhistory-manila-galleon https://www.worldhistory.org/Manila_Galleon/; [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-manila-galleon https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manila_galleon
links: east-india-company-1600, voc-1602

[E-W1-024-02] W1 1565 financial TB
Manila Galleon 1565: silver-to-China conveyor belt
The Manila-Acapulco galleon trade begins in 1565, once Alonso de Arellano and Andres de Urdaneta discover the eastward return route (the tornaviaje) across the North Pacific - the breakthrough enabling a sustained crossing. For 250 years (1565-1815) the galleons carry Asian silk, spices and porcelain east to Acapulco and return loaded with American silver. It is the first sustained trans-Pacific trade route and the Pacific leg of the global silver-extraction system - how American silver reaches Asia - the Spanish crown-monopoly variant of the joint-stock/chartered-monopoly trade technology that the EIC and VOC develop into corporate form.
sources: [B live_verified] src-worldhistory-manila-galleon https://www.worldhistory.org/Manila_Galleon/; [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-manila-galleon https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manila_galleon
links: east-india-company-1600, voc-1602

[E-W1-025-01] W1 1568 military TB
Dutch Revolt 1568: Amsterdam capitalization pipeline opens
The Seventeen Provinces of the Netherlands revolt against Philip II of Spain from 1568, opening the Eighty Years' War (1568-1648) that will produce the Dutch Republic. The triggers are heavy taxation and the persecution of Protestants; the revolt's naval arm, the Watergeuzen or 'Sea Beggars', seizes towns in Holland and Zeeland from 1572. The trigger-note this audit tracks: the long war fragments the Spanish/Habsburg financial position and begins the shift of financial gravity toward Amsterdam - the start (not the completion) of the Amsterdam-finance development that the VOC and the Sephardic-Amsterdam middleware later carry.
sources: [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-eighty-years-war https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eighty_Years%27_War
links: spanish-armada-1588, voc-1602

[E-W1-025-02] W1 1568 financial TB
Amsterdam begins ascent as alternative commercial-financial hub to Antwerp
framework: Amsterdam commercial-financial hub capitalization pipeline; upstream of VOC 1602 and 17th-century Dutch corporate architecture
assessment: Weight: Proven | Warrant: Closed
sources: [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-eighty-years-war https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eighty_Years%27_War
links: spanish-armada-1588, voc-1602

[E-W1-026-01] W1 1577-80 military TA
Drake circumnavigation: state-privateer joint venture template
Francis Drake circumnavigates the globe in the Golden Hind between 1577 and 1580 - the first expedition commander to survive the entire circumnavigation (Elcano had captained the Victoria home in 1522) - raiding Spanish shipping and ports along the way and capturing treasure ships (in March 1579 the silver-laden Nuestra Senora de la Concepcion off Peru). The loot is perhaps 600,000 pounds (estimates run from 300,000 to 1,500,000), at least the whole annual revenue of England; the queen, who had secretly invested and instructed him to take Spanish treasure, receives 160,000 pounds and knights him in 1581. It is state-sanctioned privateering - the crown-licensed agent draining Spanish silver, the same private-enterprise-plus-crown-license pattern as Roanoke and the chartered companies.
sources: [A live_verified] src-drake-world-encompassed-1628 https://nationalhumanitiescenter.org/pds/amerbegin/contact/text5/drake.pdf; [B live_verified] src-worldhistory-francis-drake https://www.worldhistory.org/Francis_Drake/; [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-circumnavigation https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circumnavigation; [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-juan-sebastian-elcano https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Sebasti%C3%A1n_Elcano; [A live_verified] src-keynes-treatise-money-1930 https://archive.org/details/in.ernet.dli.2015.150075; [B live_verified] src-worldhistory-drake-circumnavigation https://www.worldhistory.org/article/1574/francis-drakes-circumnavigation-of-the-globe/
links: roanoke-1585, east-india-company-1600

[E-W1-027-01] W1 1580 legal TB
Iberian Union 1580-1640: peak Iberian consolidation as prelude to displacement
In 1580 Philip II of Spain succeeds to the Portuguese crown - after the death of King Sebastian at Alcacer Quibir (1578) and the short reign of Cardinal Henry - marching into Portugal and being recognized as king by the Cortes of Tomar in 1581. The Iberian Union (1580-1640) puts one monarch over both global colonial empires. The framework reading (tier C, inference, not asserted from the succession itself): the union consolidates the two colonial franchises under one crown, bringing the Tordesillas-divided halves of the non-Christian world under single management.
sources: [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-iberian-union https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iberian_Union

[E-W1-028-01] W1 1581 legal TB
Levant Company 1581: joint-stock corporate form scaled
Elizabeth I charters the Turkey Company in 1581 for English trade with the Ottoman Empire; in 1592 it merges with the Venice Company (1583) to form the Levant Company, a chartered joint-stock monopoly on Levantine trade. Levant Company merchants finance the 1591 Lancaster voyage that directly precedes the East India Company (1600): the corporate-form template scales Muscovy (1555) to Turkey/Levant (1581/1592) to the EIC (1600). Direct organizational ancestor.
sources: [B live_verified] src-levant-company-1581 https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levant_Company
links: muscovy-company-1555-joint-stock-precursor-1555, east-india-company-1600

[E-W1-028-02] W1 1581 financial TB
Second English joint-stock; Mediterranean trade with Ottoman Empire
1581/1592: the Turkey Company (1581) and the Venice Company (1583) merge into the Levant Company (1592), a chartered joint-stock monopoly on Ottoman trade. Its merchants finance the 1591 Lancaster voyage that directly precedes the East India Company (1600) - the Muscovy to Levant to EIC joint-stock lineage.
sources: [B live_verified] src-levant-company-1581 https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levant_Company
links: muscovy-company-1555-joint-stock-precursor-1555, east-india-company-1600

[E-W1-029-01] W1 1585 military TB
Roanoke 1585-1590: English challenge to Iberian monopoly begins on the ground
Elizabeth I grants Sir Walter Raleigh a patent in 1584 to settle 'remote, heathen and barbarous lands ... not actually possessed of any Christian Prince' (the terra-nullius/heathen-land framing). The first colony (1585, Ralph Lane) fails and is evacuated by Drake in 1586; the second (1587, John White) - the 'Lost Colony', birthplace of Virginia Dare - is found deserted in 1590 ('Croatoan' carved), its relief delayed by the 1588 Armada. Roanoke was a privateering base as much as a settlement, 'ideally suited ... to prey upon Spanish treasure ships', financed by combining colonization with privateering - the private-enterprise-plus-crown-license model of the Capitulations and the chartered companies, and Discovery-Doctrine logic applied to English settlement.

[E-W1-030-01] W1 1588 military TB
Spanish Armada 1588: naval displacement begins
In 1588 Philip II sends the Spanish Armada - about 132 ships and some 24,000 men - to invade England, depose Elizabeth I, and reverse the English Reformation, counting on English Catholics. The faster English fleet defeats it at the Battle of Gravelines, and continuing storms scatter it around Scotland and Ireland; only half returns to Spain. The Dutch blockade of Parma's army in the Netherlands is a decisive factor. The Armada's failure is the strategic turning point that checks Spanish power and opens the way for English Atlantic expansion - including the colonization it delays the same year at Roanoke.
sources: [B live_verified] src-worldhistory-spanish-armada https://www.worldhistory.org/Spanish_Armada/
links: roanoke-1585, dutch-revolt-1568

[E-W1-031-01] W1 1594 military TB
Nine Years War 1594-1603: Iberian classification template redeployed on Ireland
Hugh O'Neill (Earl of Tyrone) and Hugh Roe O'Donnell lead the Nine Years' War (1594-1603), primarily in Ulster, against the Elizabethan conquest of Ireland. Spanish aid lands at Kinsale in 1601 but the war is decided for the English there; defeat leads to the Flight of the Earls (1607), the confiscation of the earls' lands (1608), and the Plantation of Ulster. The tier-calibrated framework note: this runs the conquest-confiscation-settler-plantation template on a European Christian (Irish Catholic) population - the Software-Neutrality principle (the code runs on Europeans) - but the mechanism is Anglo-plantation, distinct from the Iberian blood-classification line. The war is the trigger; the taggable classification node is the Plantation of Ulster (1609), not yet built.
sources: [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-nine-years-war-ireland https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nine_Years%27_War_(Ireland)

[E-W1-032-01] W1 1600 legal TA MILESTONE
East India Company 1600: joint-stock corporate form fully installed
Elizabeth I grants a royal charter on 31 December 1600 to the Governor and Company of Merchants of London Trading into the East Indies - a chartered company (it traded through separately subscribed voyages until 1612 and raised a permanent joint stock in 1657) with a monopoly (initially 15 years) on all English trade east of the Cape of Good Hope. World History Encyclopedia says the Company was 'permitted to wage war' and to 'exercise sovereignty in the name of the English Crown'; the 1600 charter itself grants neither. It allows ships 'well furnished with Ordnance, and other Munition for their Defence'; the appointment of governors comes with Charles II's charter of 1661, and the rights to make war and peace and form alliances with his acts around 1670. The corpus reads the Company as the corporate-as-quasi-state instrument - a private mercantilist corporation exercising sovereign functions under state charter, the corporate-grid technology in developed form and twin to the VOC (1602).
sources: [A live_verified] src-eic-first-charter-1600 https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Page:A_Collection_of_Charters_and_Statutes_relating_to_the_East_India_Company.pdf/9; [B live_verified] src-worldhistory-east-india-company https://www.worldhistory.org/East_India_Company/; [A live_verified] src-shaw-eic-charters-1887 https://archive.org/details/dli.ministry.01275; [A live_verified] src-eic-charters-collection-1817-p11 https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Page:A_Collection_of_Charters_and_Statutes_relating_to_the_East_India_Company.pdf/11; [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-east-india-company https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_India_Company; [B live_verified] src-britannica-east-india-company https://www.britannica.com/topic/East-India-Company

[E-W1-033-01] W1 1602 legal TB MILESTONE
VOC 1602: hinge to W2 Corporate Grid; Amsterdam Tier 1 institutionalized
The Staten-Generaal charters the Vereenigde Oostindische Compagnie (VOC) on 20 March 1602 - a 21-year charter, 6.4 million guilders in capital, an exclusive monopoly on Dutch trade in Asia. It is the world's first publicly traded company (open to public share issues) and, though chartered for trade, 'became a colonial power ... with the right to make treaties, build fortifications, and conduct military operations.' The VOC is the fully-developed corporate-grid form - a chartered joint-stock monopoly wielding sovereign functions - the twin developed corporation to the EIC (1600); together they are the corporate technology the Manila galleon's crown-monopoly variant precedes.
sources: [B live_verified] src-worldhistory-voc https://www.worldhistory.org/Dutch_East_India_Company/
links: east-india-company-1600, manila-galleon-1565

[E-W1-034-01] W1 1550 population TC
The Moor/native juridical seam: invincible ignorance vs culpable rejection (audit inference)
The audit's forensic inference, not a quotation. The Salamanca scholastic just-war tradition holds two juridical categories with different war-standing: the invincibly ignorant, who never met the Gospel (the Amerindians), and the culpable rejecter, who knew and refused Christ (the Moors). Valladolid works inside this tradition without stating the line, and Sepulveda's Democrates Secundus explicitly excludes mere disbelief as a just cause; the Requerimiento asserts only blanket papal jurisdiction. The distinction is drawn in no reached primary. The audit synthesizes it from the tradition (Vitoria, De Indis; Pagden, The Fall of Natural Man), held with confidence as tier-C inference, not primary text. It is the category the managers' own theology drew, the same argument as the far-timeline Moor-to-Negro reclassification overwrite.
sources: [A citation_only] src-vitoria-de-indis
links: 1924-triple-lock-reclassification-chain-step-1924
threads: T-SOFTWARE-NEUTRALITY

[E-W1-035-01] W1 1564 military TA
Hawkins 1564: the Crown commits a warship and the interloping escalates
Sparke sailed on the second voyage and wrote its narrative. Hakluyt's title names Hawkins captain of the Jesus of Lubeck, "one of Her Majesty's ships", so the Crown's stake is now a naval vessel rather than a private share as in 1562. Sparke records the company "pretending to have taken negroes there perforce", and one landing cost "seven of our best men" with "27 of our men hurt".
sources: [A live_verified] src-sparke-hawkins-1564 https://archive.org/details/voyagesofhawkins00hakluoft

[E-W1-036-01] W1 1562 military TA
Hawkins 1562: England's entry into the slave trade, privately financed and taken by the sword
Hakluyt's first-voyage narrative records three privately provided ships and a London syndicate who "became liberal contributors and adventurers in the action", among them Benjamin Gonson, Treasurer of the Navy and Hawkins's father-in-law. At Tagarin he got people into his possession "partly by the sword and partly by other means, to the number of 300 Negros at the least", and sold across three ports of Hispaniola.

framework: The state-privateer joint venture in its earliest English form, and the one where the state is present as private persons rather than as a vessel: the Treasurer and the Surveyor of the Navy are adventurers in a slaving voyage two years before the Crown lends a warship

[E-W2-001-01] W2 1600 financial TB
East India Company chartered
English EIC: Crown-licensed monopoly on Asian trade, chartered by Elizabeth I on 31 December 1600. Thomas Smythe is already governor of the Muscovy and Levant companies when he becomes the EIC's first governor, a post he holds to 1621 apart from a break in 1605-07. He obtains the Virginia Company charter in 1609 and serves as its treasurer until 1619. For two decades the EIC's general assemblies meet in his own house at Philpot Lane.
sources: [B live_verified] src-encyc-va-smythe https://encyclopediavirginia.org/entries/smythe-sir-thomas-ca-1558-1625/; [B citation_only] src-britannica-smythe https://www.britannica.com/money/Thomas-Smythe; [B citation_only] src-hop-smythe-1558-1625 https://www.historyofparliamentonline.org/volume/1604-1629/member/smythe-sir-thomas-1558-1625
links: east-india-company-1600, levant-company-1581-joint-stock-corporate-1581, voc-1602
threads: T-FRANCHISE-TRANSFER

[E-W2-001-02] W2 1600b catastrophe TB
Huaynaputina eruption → global famine
The largest recorded eruption in South American history, VEI 6, from 19 February 1600. Its sulphur aerosols cooled the Northern Hemisphere to its coldest summer in six centuries the next year, wrecked harvests across Europe, Asia and the Americas, and helped trigger the Russian famine of 1601-1603. A global exogenous shock at the window’s opening, external to the American Levant and to the Siphon.
sources: [A citation_only] src-desilva-zielinski-huaynaputina; [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-huaynaputina https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huaynaputina
links: port-royal-earthquake-1692, lisbon-earthquake-1755

[E-W2-002-01] W2 1606 financial TA
Virginia Company chartered
King James I, April 10. Private corporation granted permanent military authority to "expulse and resist" anyone without license. All land held "in free and common soccage" from the Manor at East-Greenwich - the colonists are TENANTS, never owners. Nemo dat: tenants cannot grant allodial title downstream.
sources: [A live_verified] src-avalon-virginia-charter-1606 https://avalon.law.yale.edu/17th_century/va01.asp; [B citation_only] src-robinson-mother-earth-land-grants; [C citation_only] src-corporate-caselaw-layer-1602-1713

threads: T-DISCOVERY-DOCTRINE

[E-W2-002-02] W2 1606 legal TA
Virginia Charter - "Not possessed by any Christian Prince"
Non-Christians rendered juridically invisible. Same logic as Dum Diversas: non-Christian = unoccupied = available. "Infidels and Savages" in "Darkness and miserable Ignorance." The Papal Bull logic imported into English corporate law.
sources: [A live_verified] src-avalon-virginia-charter-1606 https://avalon.law.yale.edu/17th_century/va01.asp; [B citation_only] src-robinson-mother-earth-land-grants; [C citation_only] src-corporate-caselaw-layer-1602-1713

[E-W2-002-03] W2 1606 land TA
American Levant coast claimed for Crown
"All along the Sea Coasts, between four and thirty Degrees of Northerly Latitude." Preemptive Crown title - "either appertaining unto us" - claims the land belongs to James I before any settlement or purchase.
sources: [A live_verified] src-avalon-virginia-charter-1606 https://avalon.law.yale.edu/17th_century/va01.asp; [B citation_only] src-robinson-mother-earth-land-grants; [C citation_only] src-corporate-caselaw-layer-1602-1713

[E-W2-003-01] W2 1607 violence TB
Jamestown established
First permanent English settlement in the American Levant. Built on Powhatan Confederacy territory. "Starving Time" (1609-10): 80% mortality. The franchise nearly fails. Will be restocked with indentured labor and then classified labor.
sources: [B live_verified] src-jamestown-1607 https://www.britannica.com/place/Jamestown-Colony

[E-W2-004-01] W2 1609 financial TB
Bank of Amsterdam (Wisselbank)
First public bank in Northern Europe. Standardizes currency exchange. Eliminates debasement. Amsterdam becomes global settlement hub. The Sephardic financial template institutionalized. Isaac Le Maire conducts world’s first short sale on VOC stock.
sources: [B live_verified] src-bank-of-amsterdam-1609 https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_Amsterdam

[E-W2-004-02] W2 1609b land TB
Henry Hudson claims Hudson River
Henry Hudson, sailing for the Dutch East India Company aboard the Halve Maen, enters the river that now bears his name in the summer of 1609, reaching near present-day Albany before the shallows turn him back. He finds no passage to Asia, but his survey of the coast founds the Dutch claim to the region that becomes New Netherland.
sources: [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-halve-maen https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halve_Maen

[E-W2-005-01] W2 1619 population TA
First Africans at Jamestown
“20. and odd Negroes” taken from the São João Bautista, a Portuguese slaver out of São Paulo de Luanda bound for Vera Cruz with 350 captives, 200 of them licensed under asiento to Seville investors. Privateers White Lion and Treasurer, both out of Flushing under foreign letters of marque, took about 60 off Campeche. White Lion reached Point Comfort late August 1619; Governor Yeardley and Cape Merchant Abraham Piersey bought them for victuals.
sources: [A citation_only] src-sluiter-20-odd-negroes-1997; [A live_verified] src-encyc-va-first-africans https://encyclopediavirginia.org/entries/africans-virginias-first/; [B live_verified] src-austin-1619-virginias-first-africans https://hampton.gov/DocumentCenter/View/24075/; [B live_verified] src-hampton-1619-african-arrival https://www.hampton.gov/3584/1619-African-Arrival-Exhibit

[E-W2-005-02] W2 1619 information TA
John Rolfe records the arrival
"About the latter end of August, a Dutch man of Warr… brought not anything but 20 and odd Negroes." The word "Negroes" enters the English colonial archive. First documented use in Virginia records.
sources: [A citation_only] src-sluiter-20-odd-negroes-1997; [A live_verified] src-encyc-va-first-africans https://encyclopediavirginia.org/entries/africans-virginias-first/; [B live_verified] src-austin-1619-virginias-first-africans https://hampton.gov/DocumentCenter/View/24075/; [B live_verified] src-hampton-1619-african-arrival https://www.hampton.gov/3584/1619-African-Arrival-Exhibit

[E-W2-005-03] W2 1619b innovation TB
Virginia tobacco economy established
John Rolfe’s sweeter Orinoco strain, first cultivated around 1611 and exported from 1612, makes tobacco Virginia’s first profitable export: 20,000 pounds shipped in 1617, 40,000 in 1618. The cash crop drives the colony’s hunger for labour, indentured then enslaved. The row is filed at 1619 with the first Africans, though the economy it names was established several years earlier.
sources: [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-john-rolfe https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Rolfe
links: jamestown-established-1607, barbados-sugar-revolution-1640
threads: T-THREE-TRACK-LABOR

[E-W2-006-01] W2 1621 financial TB
Dutch West India Company (WIC) chartered
Chartered 3 June 1621; founder Joannes de Laet; five chambers (Amsterdam, Hoorn, Rotterdam, Groningen, Middelburg) under the Heeren XIX. Held the Atlantic monopoly on trade and warfare 1624-1674, reinstated 1674-1738, the entity surviving to 1791. Shipped at least 85,000 enslaved people before 1674 and at least 180,000 after, both floors, so at least 265,000 in total, about a quarter of all Dutch slave trade. Supplied Spanish colonies via Curaçao as entrepôt, an indirect stake in the Asiento.
sources: [A citation_only] src-postma-dutch-atlantic-slave-trade; [A citation_only] src-den-heijer-geschiedenis-wic; [B live_verified] src-low-countries-wic-slave-trade https://www.the-low-countries.com/article/approved-by-the-bible-the-slave-trade-of-the-dutch-west-india-company/; [B live_verified] src-project-manifest-wic https://www.projectmanifest.eu/the-wic-the-dutch-west-india-company-en-fr/
links: voc-1602, asiento-first-1518, piet-hein-captures-spanish-silver-fleet-1628
threads: T-ASIENTO, T-FRANCHISE-TRANSFER

[E-W2-006-02] W2 1621 legal TB
WIC Charter = Asiento pipeline formalized
Monopoly over Dutch participation in Atlantic slave trade. Military, judicial, and administrative powers granted. Same charter model as VOC/EIC/Virginia Co. The extraction function now has competing corporate franchises in every zone.
sources: [A citation_only] src-postma-dutch-atlantic-slave-trade; [A citation_only] src-den-heijer-geschiedenis-wic; [B live_verified] src-low-countries-wic-slave-trade https://www.the-low-countries.com/article/approved-by-the-bible-the-slave-trade-of-the-dutch-west-india-company/; [B live_verified] src-project-manifest-wic https://www.projectmanifest.eu/the-wic-the-dutch-west-india-company-en-fr/

[E-W2-007-01] W2 1624 legal TB
Virginia becomes Royal Colony
Virginia Company charter revoked. Colony transferred from private corporation to direct Crown control. The franchise failed as a company; the Crown takes over directly. Same extraction, new management letterhead.
sources: [B live_verified] src-virginia-royal-colony-1624 https://encyclopediavirginia.org/entries/virginia-company-of-london/

[E-W2-007-02] W2 1624b land TB
Dutch establish Fort Orange (Albany)
In 1624 the Dutch West India Company builds Fort Orange on the Hudson at present-day Albany - the first permanent Dutch settlement in New Netherland, replacing the abandoned Fort Nassau. It is built on Mahican land without consent (unlike later purchase-based settlement) and operates as a fur-trade post at the Iroquois/Algonquian crossroads: the WIC's land-claim-and-extraction node in North America.
sources: [B live_verified] src-fort-orange-1624 https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Orange_(New_Netherland)

[E-W2-008-01] W2 1628 financial TB
Piet Hein captures Spanish Silver Fleet
WIC admiral seizes entire Spanish treasure fleet off Cuba. ~11.5 million guilders - enough to finance the conquest of Brazil. Spanish Potosí silver redirected to Dutch accounts. The Staffing Agencies pirate each other’s revenue streams.
sources: [B live_verified] src-piet-hein-1628 https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piet_Pieterszoon_Hein

[E-W2-009-01] W2 1636b financial TB
Tulip Mania
Dutch tulip futures peak in late 1636 and collapse in February 1637. The image of nationwide ruin is folklore: Goldgar’s archival work finds the trade confined to a fairly small circle of wealthy merchants and craftsmen, fewer than half a dozen in real financial trouble, and, money having largely not yet changed hands, the crash cost almost no one anything. The shock was to a network of values, not to the economy.
sources: [A citation_only] src-goldgar-tulipmania; [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-tulip-mania https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulip_mania
links: bank-of-amsterdam-wisselbank-1609, first-government-bonds-issued-1693, bubble-act-1720
threads: T-MERCHANT-BANKER-CONSTANT

[E-W2-009-02] W2 1636 violence TB
Pequot War
The Pequot War (1636-1638): at the Mystic Massacre (26 May 1637) English colonists with Mohegan and Narragansett allies burn the Pequot fort, killing over 700, mostly women and children. Modern scholarship holds the war's causes were a pretext for English expansion - a land-clearing operation. Survivors are sold into slavery in the West Indies and Bermuda or given as slaves to the allied tribes: the war is the trigger, a Track-C-style labour export, not itself the classification instrument (that is the Treaty of Hartford).
sources: [B live_verified] src-pequot-war-hartford https://www.worldhistory.org/Pequot_War/
links: 1924-triple-lock-reclassification-chain-step-1924, nine-years-war-ireland-1594
threads: T-THREE-TRACK-LABOR

[E-W2-009-03] W2 1636 population TB
Pequot survivors enslaved & exported
The Treaty of Hartford (21 September 1638) legally abolishes the Pequot as a people: by the treaty's own words the Pequots 'shall no more be called Pequots but Narragansetts and Mohegans', and the victors 'shall not suffer them for to live in the country that was formerly theirs but now is the English by conquest' (taking their enslavers' names, barred from their land). It is a reclassification-by-legal-erasure operation - abolishing a specific polity's Noun by treaty (Software-Neutrality, tagged on the name-abolition; now resting on the treaty primary). It is the direct structural ancestor of Plecker's 1924 abolition of 'Moor' from Virginia records: the same legal-name-erasure ~286 years apart, the Noun-to-Adjective spine.
sources: [B live_verified] src-pequot-war-hartford https://www.worldhistory.org/Pequot_War/; [B live_verified] src-treaty-hartford-1638 https://venturesmithcolonialct.org/library/treaty-of-hartford-1638/
links: 1924-triple-lock-reclassification-chain-step-1924, nine-years-war-ireland-1594
threads: T-THREE-TRACK-LABOR, T-SOFTWARE-NEUTRALITY, T-NOUN-TO-ADJECTIVE

[E-W2-009-04] W2 1636 land TB
Pequot territory seized
By the Treaty of Hartford the surviving Pequots are divided among the victors, but the LAND goes to the English alone: the country “that was formerly theirs but now is the English by conquest”, and “neither shall the Narragansetts nor Mohegans possess any part of the Pequot Country without leave from the English”. Survivors are forbidden to return to their ancestral lands and forbidden the Pequot name. Land-clearing completed: the war removes the population, the treaty extinguishes the claim.
sources: [B live_verified] src-pequot-war-hartford https://www.worldhistory.org/Pequot_War/; [B live_verified] src-treaty-hartford-1638 https://venturesmithcolonialct.org/library/treaty-of-hartford-1638/
links: 1924-triple-lock-reclassification-chain-step-1924, nine-years-war-ireland-1594
threads: T-THREE-TRACK-LABOR

[E-W2-010-01] W2 1640 population TB
John Punch case
Virginia. Three servants escape: two European, one African. Europeans receive extended indentures. The African (John Punch) receives lifetime servitude. First documented case of racial differential sentencing in English colonies. The classification tested as criminal punishment.
sources: [A needs_verification] src-encyc-va-servants-slaves https://encyclopediavirginia.org/entries/servants-and-slaves-in-colonial-virginia/; [C citation_only] src-corporate-caselaw-layer-1602-1713

threads: T-THREE-TRACK-LABOR, T-WHITENING-PROJECT

[E-W2-010-02] W2 1640 legal TB
Racial sentencing differential established
The precedent: same crime, different punishment based on classification. Not yet property law - still criminal law. Johnson v. Casor (1655) will convert this from criminal to civil.
sources: [A needs_verification] src-encyc-va-servants-slaves https://encyclopediavirginia.org/entries/servants-and-slaves-in-colonial-virginia/; [C citation_only] src-corporate-caselaw-layer-1602-1713

threads: T-THREE-TRACK-LABOR, T-WHITENING-PROJECT

[E-W2-010-03] W2 1640b innovation TB
Sugar revolution begins, Barbados
Sugar cane arrives from Dutch Brazil around 1640 and remakes Barbados. Within two years it is the focus of the economy; small tobacco holdings are consolidated into large plantations, and the labour demand shifts decisively to enslaved Africans, soon three for every planter. The sugar revolution is the economic pressure behind the island’s slave codes: the 1661 Act that first comprehensively governs the enslaved is written to control the workforce sugar requires.
sources: [A citation_only] src-dunn-sugar-and-slaves; [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-history-of-barbados https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Barbados
links: virginia-tobacco-economy-1619, barbados-slave-code-1661, code-noir-1685

[E-W2-011-01] W2 1655b violence TB
Cromwell seizes Jamaica
Western Design. Same merchant network (Noell/Povey/Carvajal) runs both the court case and the military operation. Legal test + military seizure synchronized within two months.
sources: [A citation_only] src-pestana-english-conquest-jamaica; [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-western-design https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Design

[E-W2-011-02] W2 1655 legal TA
Johnson v. Casor - “Negro” = Property
Northampton County Court. First civil case declaring a person property WITHOUT A CRIME. Anthony Johnson (free Black landholder) uses the "Negro" classification to claim Casor as property. The Asiento’s inventory classification now functional in English courts. The Noun (Free Landholder) deploys the Adjective (Negro) - and the Adjective will later consume the Noun.
sources: [A citation_only] src-northampton-order-book-1655; [A needs_verification] src-encyc-va-servants-slaves https://encyclopediavirginia.org/entries/servants-and-slaves-in-colonial-virginia/; [B live_verified] src-russell-free-negro-virginia-1916 https://archive.org/details/freenegro00russrich; [B citation_only] src-schermerhorn-anthony-johnson-2019; [C citation_only] src-corporate-caselaw-layer-1602-1713

[E-W2-011-03] W2 1655b land TB
Jamaica becomes English territory
Seized from Spain. Will become the Caribbean hub for the English slave trade - and Port Royal becomes richest city in the Americas (until it sinks in 1692).
sources: [A citation_only] src-pestana-english-conquest-jamaica; [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-western-design https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Design

[E-W2-011-04] W2 1655b financial TB
Western Design financed as a state venture
Cromwell's Western Design, in the Anglo-Spanish War of 1654 to 1660, organised under Cromwell and John Desborough with command split between Penn and Venables. The Hispaniola attack failed in April 1655; Jamaica was taken between 19 and 27 May. Spain formally ceded the island in 1670. Of little economic value at seizure, and strategically a dagger at the Spanish empire.
sources: [A citation_only] src-pestana-english-conquest-jamaica; [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-western-design https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Design
links: iberian-union-1580, maroon-communities-forming-counter-move-1655, royal-african-company-chartered-1672
threads: T-FRANCHISE-TRANSFER, T-ASIENTO

[E-W2-012-01] W2 ~1655 counter-move TB MILESTONE
★ Maroon communities forming - Counter-Move
Across the Caribbean and the American interior, people who escape the classification form independent, self-governing communities. In Jamaica, when the Spanish flee the English conquest of 1655, the people they held take their own freedom and coalesce in the interior as the Maroons (from the Spanish cimarrón, wild, untamed). They hold their ground by force until the treaties of 1739 and 1740 acknowledge their autonomy and land, in exchange for military service and the return of later runaways.
sources: [A citation_only] src-campbell-maroons-jamaica; [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-jamaican-maroons https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamaican_Maroons
links: cromwell-seizes-jamaica-1655, fort-mose-1738
threads: T-COLLAPSE-SURVIVAL-N1

[E-W2-012-02] W2 ~1655 counter-move TB
★ Maroon treaties = sovereignty acknowledged
When colonial governments sign TREATIES with Maroon communities, they acknowledge that these are not runaway slaves - they are treaty-capable polities. Treaty language implies sovereignty. Jamaica’s 1739 treaty grants Maroon territory, internal self-governance, and freedom from colonial law. The same treaty mechanism as the 1787 Morocco Treaty - the Managers negotiate because the military option has failed.
sources: [A citation_only] src-campbell-maroons-jamaica; [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-jamaican-maroons https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamaican_Maroons
links: cromwell-seizes-jamaica-1655, fort-mose-1738
threads: T-COLLAPSE-SURVIVAL-N1

[E-W2-012-03] W2 ~1655 counter-move TB
★ Maroon territorial sovereignty
Blue Mountains (Jamaica), interior Suriname, Quilombo dos Palmares (Brazil). The Noun carves out physical territory the Adjective cannot reach. Some communities persist into the present day. The classification fails wherever it cannot deploy sufficient violence to enforce it.
sources: [A citation_only] src-campbell-maroons-jamaica; [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-jamaican-maroons https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jamaican_Maroons
links: cromwell-seizes-jamaica-1655, fort-mose-1738
threads: T-COLLAPSE-SURVIVAL-N1

[E-W2-013-01] W2 1656 legal TA
Elizabeth Key lawsuit - THE EXIT DOOR THAT TRIGGERS PARTUS
Daughter of English planter Thomas Key and an enslaved African woman. When John Mottrom's estate is settled in 1655 she is inventoried as “Elizabeth the Negro woman & her sonne”. She sues on three grounds: paternal status at common law, Christian baptism, and an expired nine-year indenture. A Northumberland jury frees her 20 January 1656; the Council reverses 12 March; the Assembly finds “the Child of a Woman slave begott by a free-man ought to bee free”. She wins 21 July 1656.
sources: [A live_verified] src-dvb-elizabeth-key https://www.lva.virginia.gov/public/dvb/bio.asp?b=Key_Elizabeth_fl_1655-1660; [B citation_only] src-blackpast-elizabeth-key https://blackpast.org/african-american-history/grinstead-elizabeth-key-1630/; [B citation_only] src-nyhs-wams-elizabeth-key https://wams.nyhistory.org/life-story/elizabeth-key-grinstead/
links: john-punch-case-1640, partus-sequitur-ventrem-1662, virginia-baptism-not-freedom-1667
threads: T-WHITENING-PROJECT

[E-W2-014-01] W2 1660 financial TA
Company of Royal Adventurers
1660: Charles II grants the Company of Royal Adventurers trading into Africa its original charter, re-incorporated 10 January 1663. Governor: James, Duke of York, the King's brother. The Crown enters the African trade at the ownership level, a generation before the RAC. After heavy wartime losses the company surrenders its charter, and the Royal African Company supersedes it in 1672.
sources: [A live_verified] src-csp-colonial-awi-vol7-rac-charter https://www.british-history.ac.uk/cal-state-papers/colonial/america-west-indies/vol7/pp404-417; [A citation_only] src-davies-royal-african-company
links: asiento-first-1518, royal-african-company-chartered-1672, rac-monopoly-broken-slave-trade-deregulated-1698
threads: T-ASIENTO

[E-W2-014-02] W2 1660b legal TA
Navigation Acts
Navigation Act, 13 September 1660. Enumerated colonial goods, sugar, tobacco, cotton, indigo, ginger and dyewoods, may ship only to England or English colonies, in English-built ships crewed three-quarters English. The mercantile frame that funnels colonial trade through England: colonial wealth routed to English shippers and merchants, colonial economies subordinated to the imperial account. Basis of nearly two centuries of trade regulation.
sources: [A live_verified] src-navigation-act-1660-statute https://www.british-history.ac.uk/statutes-realm/vol5/pp246-250; [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-navigation-acts https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navigation_Acts
links: east-india-company-chartered-1600, company-of-royal-adventurers-1660, rac-monopoly-broken-slave-trade-deregulated-1698
threads: T-FRANCHISE-TRANSFER

[E-W2-015-01] W2 1661 legal TA
Barbados Slave Code
September 1661, twenty-three clauses: “An Act for the better ordering and governing of Negroes,” the first comprehensive slave code in the English colonies. Its preamble puts the enslaved outside common law as a “heathenish, brutish, and uncertain dangerous kind of people,” to be protected as “other goods and chattels.” The same assembly passed a parallel Christian Servants code the same day. The template travels: Jamaica 1664, revised 1684, South Carolina 1691.
sources: [A citation_only] src-barbados-negroes-act-1661-pro; [A live_verified] src-unb-slavery-freedom-barbados-1661 https://slaveryandfreedomlaws.lib.unb.ca/laws/barbados-1661; [A citation_only] src-rugemer-mastery-race-slave-codes; [B citation_only] src-engerman-drescher-paquette-slavery
links: barbados-sugar-revolution-1640, code-noir-1685, virginia-slave-codes-1705, sc-negro-act-1740
threads: T-THREE-TRACK-LABOR

[E-W2-016-01] W2 1662 population TA
Partus Sequitur Ventrem - Biological Lock
Virginia colonial statute, Act XII of December 1662. “All children borne in this country shalbe held bond or free only according to the condition of the mother”, reversing the common-law presumption that status descended from the father. The classification tested in Johnson v. Casor (1655) becomes hereditary, passing by birth rather than by contract, crime, or conversion. Follows the Elizabeth Key suit of 1656, which tested the paternity rule the act abolishes.
sources: [A live_verified] src-va-act-xii-1662-partus https://encyclopediavirginia.org/entries/negro-womens-children-to-serve-according-to-the-condition-of-the-mother-1662/; [A live_verified] src-dvb-elizabeth-key https://www.lva.virginia.gov/public/dvb/bio.asp?b=Key_Elizabeth_fl_1655-1660; [C citation_only] src-corporate-caselaw-layer-1602-1713
links: elizabeth-key-lawsuit-the-exit-door-1656, casta-system-1560s, virginia-code-1682, virginia-slave-codes-1705
threads: T-WHITENING-PROJECT

[E-W2-016-02] W2 1662 legal TA
Partus codified
Act XII of the Virginia Assembly. "All children borne in this country shalbe held bond or free only according to the condition of the mother." Overrides 600 years of English precedent. The classification is now biologically heritable.
sources: [A live_verified] src-va-act-xii-1662-partus https://encyclopediavirginia.org/entries/negro-womens-children-to-serve-according-to-the-condition-of-the-mother-1662/; [A live_verified] src-dvb-elizabeth-key https://www.lva.virginia.gov/public/dvb/bio.asp?b=Key_Elizabeth_fl_1655-1660; [C citation_only] src-corporate-caselaw-layer-1602-1713

[E-W2-016-03] W2 1662b information TB
Schott Physica Curiosa - JESUIT KNOWLEDGE INVERSION
Gaspar Schott, SJ, Physica Curiosa (Würzburg, 1662): twelve books, the first six on the miraculous, a section among them on human and animal monsters, the last six on natural marvels, densely illustrated with dozens of engraved plates. Schott is a compiler, not an originator, aggregating the existing European literature and illustration of human variation from Gesner and Aldrovandi. It appears the same decade as Partus, Barbados 1661 and the Virginia codes.
sources: [A live_verified] src-schott-physica-curiosa-1662 https://archive.org/details/chepfl-lipr-AXB32_01; [B live_verified] src-bhl-schott-monsters https://blog.biodiversitylibrary.org/2013/05/monsters-scientific-revolution-and.html; [B citation_only] src-salomao-schott-physica-curiosa-2009

[E-W2-017-01] W2 1663 financial TB
Company charter names "negro slaves" as commodity
1663 charter explicitly lists "negro slaves" as trade goods - first time in a Crown corporate charter. Asiento inventory language in English corporate instruments.

[E-W2-017-02] W2 1663 legal TB
New France becomes royal colony
18 September 1663. Louis XIV dissolves the Company of One Hundred Associates and takes New France into the royal domain, then charters the French West India Company in 1664 to run its commerce. France now builds a colonial grid parallel to England's: Canada for fur, the Caribbean for sugar, later Louisiana. That standing French infrastructure is what makes the Bourbon Mortgage of 1778 intelligible.

[E-W2-018-01] W2 1664 violence TB
English seize New Amsterdam
Renamed New York. Duke of York (RAC Governor, future James II) takes the Dutch colony. The future financial capital of the American Levant transferred from Dutch WIC to English Crown franchise - by the same man who runs the slave trade company.

[E-W2-018-02] W2 1664 land TB
New Netherland → New York
27 August 1664. Four English frigates take New Amsterdam; the whole Dutch seaboard colony, Manhattan, the Hudson Valley, the future Wall Street, becomes New York. The Articles of Capitulation preserve Dutch property and trade, so the commercial machinery carries on under English management. Confirmed at the Treaty of Breda in 1667.

[E-W2-019-01] W2 1665 catastrophe TB
Great Plague of London
1665-1666. Roughly 100,000 die, nearly a quarter of London, in eighteen months. The court and the wealthy flee, the King to Salisbury then Oxford, while the poor are held back by health certificates and refused entry to the villages beyond. A mortality-and-displacement catastrophe, the first of the two London shocks; its long consequence is the rebuilding that follows the next year's fire.
sources: [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-great-plague-london https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Plague_of_London
links: black-death-1347-51, great-fire-of-london-1666

[E-W2-019-02] W2 1665 population TB
Mass displacement from London
Entire populations flee the city. Estates abandoned. Businesses collapsed. The displaced population pool feeds the colonial pipeline - desperate people accept indenture contracts.
sources: [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-great-plague-london https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Plague_of_London
links: black-death-1347-51, great-fire-of-london-1666

[E-W2-020-01] W2 1666b catastrophe TB
Great Fire of London
13,200 houses, 87 churches destroyed. September 2-5. Combined with plague: total infrastructure reset for the City of London. The old medieval city - with its medieval records - is incinerated.
sources: [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-great-fire-london https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Fire_of_London
links: great-plague-of-london-1665, port-royal-earthquake-1692

[E-W2-020-02] W2 1666b records TB
London records destroyed
Medieval guild records, parish registers, property deeds, court records. The fire erases the documentary chain for thousands of London estates. Whoever rebuilds controls the new ledger.
sources: [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-great-fire-london https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Fire_of_London
links: great-plague-of-london-1665, port-royal-earthquake-1692

[E-W2-020-03] W2 1666 legal TA
Cestui Que Vie Act
Statute 18 Cha. 2 c. 11. Where a land tenure ran on the life of a named person (cestui que vie) who had gone overseas, those in remainder could not prove the life had ended and were kept out of the estate. The Act sets a rule of evidence: after seven years' absence with no proof of life, the person is presumed dead and the estate settles. It resolves title where a life cannot be accounted for. Passed the year London's records burned.
sources: [A live_verified] src-cestui-que-vie-act-1666 https://www.legislation.gov.uk/aep/Cha2/18-19/11/contents
links: great-plague-of-london-1665

[E-W2-021-01] W2 1670 financial TB
Hudson’s Bay Company chartered
2 May 1670. Charles II charters the Governor and Company of Adventurers of England trading into Hudson's Bay, with Prince Rupert as first governor. It receives Rupert's Land, the whole Hudson Bay watershed, roughly 1.5 million square miles, over a third of modern Canada, and a monopoly on its fur trade. The charter model, Levant to EIC to Virginia to RAC, now closes the grid's northern quarter.

Rupert’s Land - 1.5M sq miles claimed
HBC granted all lands draining into Hudson Bay. 40% of modern Canada. Largest land grant in history to a private corporation. Not purchased. Not conquered. Decreed. The land already has people.

Fur trade monopoly

[E-W2-022-01] W2 1670b legal TB
Anthony Johnson estate seized
August 1670 escheat inquisition, John Stringer escheator for Northampton and Accomack: FIFTY ACRES IN ACCOMACK COUNTY, already in the possession of Johnson's son Richard, escheated on the finding that Anthony Johnson, lately deceased, 'was a negro and by consequence an alien and for that cause the said land doth escheat'. The parcel passed to a white neighbour, George Parker. (Anthony's earlier 250-acre Northampton headright is a separate holding and is not what this inquisition took.) THE ADJECTIVE CONSUMES ITS CREATOR. Johnson used “Negro” to win Casor in 1655; now the same classification destroys his family. His sons John and Richard flee to Delaware and RENT land they once would have inherited. Complete arc: Antonio the Moor arrives as Noun → wins as Noun → deploys the Adjective → Adjective consumes the Noun → descendants reduced from landowners to tenants.

Johnson estate redistributed
Fifty acres in Accomack County transferred from a free Black family to a white settler, George Parker. This is the prototype for every future land seizure through racial reclassification.

[E-W2-023-01] W2 1672 financial TA
Royal African Company chartered
Chartered 27 September 1672 by Charles II, superseding the surrendered 1663 Company of Royal Adventurers; Governor James, Duke of York, James II from 1685. Ships roughly 5,000 enslaved people a year through the 1680s, mainly to the Caribbean and Virginia: 90,000-100,000 in the monopoly years 1672-1689, about twenty-three voyages annually. Across 1672-1731 it invested in voyages carrying 186,748 people, 16,077 of whom died at sea. Captives branded DY for the Duke, or RAC on the chest.

RAC: industrial-scale population processing
Factory system on West African coast. Captives held in coastal fortifications (Elmina, Cape Coast Castle). Shipped under standardized commercial contracts. The Asiento pipeline now operated by the English Crown at industrial volume.

Gold Coast mining + slave infrastructure
RAC simultaneously extracts gold and people from the same territory. Elmina ("The Mine") is both a gold fort and a slave holding facility. Resources and classified labor processed through the same physical infrastructure.

[E-W2-024-01] W2 1675 violence TB
King Philip’s War
1675-1678. Metacom (King Philip) leads a Wampanoag and Narragansett alliance against the New England colonies. One of the bloodiest wars per capita in American history: of roughly ninety towns more than half are attacked and twelve destroyed outright, and about one-tenth of the colonies' military-age men are killed. Indigenous power in southern New England is broken; surrendering Indians and the women and children of Mount Hope are sold into slavery.

Indigenous captives exported

Bacon’s Rebellion
Multiracial uprising of indentured servants and enslaved people against Virginia elite. White and Black laborers unite against the planter class. The Managers’ response: DIVIDE the labor pool by RACE. The 1682 and 1705 racial codes are the direct legislative response to this moment.

Jamestown burned

[E-W2-025-03] W2 1676 information TB
The lesson: unified labor = threat
Bacon’s Rebellion proves that a multiracial laboring class can overthrow the planter elite. The solution: racial law that gives poor whites a stake in the classification hierarchy. Divide the Nouns into Adjectives.

[E-W2-026-01] W2 1680 counter-move TB MILESTONE
★ Pueblo Revolt - Most successful indigenous counter-move
August 10, 1680. Popé of San Juan, a Tewa medicine man, leads an almost universal Pueblo uprising. Governor Otermín and the Spanish flee Santa Fe by 21 August. 401 settlers and 21 friars die, the friars 21 of the province's 33; the dead are roughly a quarter of New Mexico's Spanish population. Some 2,000 survivors retreat to El Paso. A Spanish force of 300 is repelled in 1681: the twelve years are defended, not merely uncontested, until Vargas reconquers in 1692.

[E-W2-026-02] W2 1680 records TB
Spanish records destroyed
The Pueblo revolutionaries deliberately burn churches, administrative buildings, and Spanish records. This is Lane 4 deployed by the Heirs: destroy the occupier’s paper trail. The archive destruction pattern reversed. When the Spanish return (1692), they have to rebuild the documentary chain from scratch.

[E-W2-026-03] W2 1680 counter-move TB
★ Spanish jurisdiction voided for 12 years
1680-1692: the Pueblo nations govern without any European presence. The Reconquista model reversed: indigenous population expels the occupier, destroys the occupier’s records, restores pre-contact governance. Proof that the classification is only as durable as the violence enforcing it.

[E-W2-026-04] W2 1680 counter-move TB
★ Pueblo territory restored
All Spanish land grants voided by force. Encomienda abolished. Missions burned. The land returns to indigenous governance for over a decade. Spanish reconquest (1692) reinstalls the framework, but the Pueblo nations negotiate from a position of strength the second time.

[E-W2-027-01] W2 1682 population TA
"Negroes, Moors, Mollattoes" merged
Virginia merges “Negroes, Moors, Mollattoes or Indians” whose “parentage and native country are not christian” into one enslaveable category, later conversion no bar. It carves out one exception: “all servants except Turkes and Moores, whilest in amity with his majesty.” The exemption turns on diplomatic amity with the Crown, not Christian status: subjects of Muslim states at peace with England are set apart where other non-Christians are not.
sources: [A live_verified] src-va-act-1682-repeal https://encyclopediavirginia.org/primary-documents/an-act-to-repeale-a-former-law-makeing-indians-and-others-ffree-1682/
links: bacons-rebellion-1676, partus-sequitur-ventrem-1662, virginia-slave-codes-1705
threads: T-WHITENING-PROJECT

[E-W2-027-02] W2 1682 legal TA
Turks and Moors in amity exempted from the statute
November 1682. The act merges "Negroes, Moors, Mollattoes or Indians" whose "parentage and native country are not christian at the time of their first purchase" into a single enslaveable category, with later conversion no bar. It carries one exception: "all servants except Turkes and Moores, whilest in amity with his majesty". The exemption does not turn on prior Christian status. It turns on political amity with the Crown.
sources: [A live_verified] src-va-act-1682-repeal https://encyclopediavirginia.org/primary-documents/an-act-to-repeale-a-former-law-makeing-indians-and-others-ffree-1682/
links: bacons-rebellion-1676, partus-sequitur-ventrem-1662, virginia-slave-codes-1705

[E-W2-028-01] W2 1685 financial TB
James II becomes King
6 February 1685. The Royal African Company's governor since 1660 becomes King of England, and keeps the post. James, Duke of York, whose initials DY are branded on the chests of the enslaved the RAC ships, some 5,000 a year in the 1680s, now holds the throne. Crown, chartered slaver and classification meet in one man, until the Glorious Revolution removes both king and governorship.
sources: [B live_verified] src-wikipedia-james-ii https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_II_of_England
links: glorious-revolution-1688
threads: T-ASIENTO

[E-W2-028-02] W2 1685b legal TA
Code Noir - France
Royal ordinance of Louis XIV, March 1685, 60 articles, commissioned from Jean-Baptiste Colbert in 1681 and drafted by the naval minister, the Marquis de Seignelay (Wikipedia). Defines the enslaved as movable, inheritable property (Art. XLIV), barred from owning goods or testifying against free persons. One ordinance sorts three populations by legal category: Article I expels all Jews as “declared enemies of the Christian name,” Articles III and V suppress Protestant worship, Article II mandates Catholic baptism for every slave. It governs slavery in the French colonies until 1789, is put back in place in 1802, and lapses with the abolition of slavery in 1848 (Wikipedia).

[E-W2-030-02] W2 1688c information TB
Germantown Quaker Petition
18 February 1688. Four Quakers at Germantown, Pennsylvania, led by Francis Daniel Pastorius, sign the first public document in the English colonies to protest slavery. On the Golden Rule they ask how Christians can hold others in a bondage they would refuse for themselves. The meeting neither adopts nor rejects it, and it disappears into the archives for 150 years.

[E-W2-037-01] W2 1696 financial TB
Great Recoinage - First Silver Demotion
Half the silver coin is clipped or forged, its bullion worth more abroad than its face at home. All silver is called in, melted and reissued; Isaac Newton, Warden of the Mint, runs the operation, and over five million pounds is struck by 1700. The recoinage is no financial success, and England drifts toward the gold standard. In the framework’s reading, silver, the people’s metal, is demoted as gold, the Managers’, is elevated.

1698. [E-W2-042-01] W2 1667 legal TA
Virginia law: baptism does not confer freedom
Virginia General Assembly, September 1667. “The conferring of baptisme doth not alter the condition of the person as to his bondage or ffreedome.” Baptism no longer frees the enslaved, so masters may “endeavour the propagation of christianity” without risking their property. The religious exit that Dum Diversas left implied, conversion out of subjugation, is closed by statute. Confirmed and folded into the comprehensive code of 1705.

[E-W2-043-01] W2 1657 legal TB
Alleged forgery - Johnson land seized
Northampton County. Edmund Scarborough, a white planter and local power broker, allegedly forged a letter in which Anthony Johnson acknowledged a debt; whether the debt was real is unknown. Johnson was illiterate and could not have written it, and did not contest the case. The court nonetheless awards Scarborough 100 acres of Johnson’s land against the alleged debt. The same court that had enforced Johnson’s property claim over Casor two years earlier now uses the “Negro” classification to strip Johnson of his own estate.

Virginia General Assembly consolidates four decades of piecemeal statute into a single slave code. “Negro” and “non-Christian” are merged into one permanent, heritable status, and baptism is confirmed as no longer an exit. Casor (1655), Partus (1662) and the 1682 merger reaches its codified form: servitude by birth, sealed against crime, contract and conversion alike.

[E-W2-044-02] W2 1705 population TB
Classification made permanent and heritable
Two ruling clauses. Religion is enumerated and then set aside: "That no negros, mulattos, or Indians, although Christians, or Jews, Moors, Mahometans, or other infidels shall at any time purchase any Christian servant". And condition is made heritable: "baptism of slaves does not exempt them from bondage; and that all children shall be bond or free, according to the condition of their mothers", legislating the partus rule of 1662.

[E-W4-002-02] W4 1790 counter-move TA MILESTONE
Sundry Free Moors petition THE ROOT PASSWORD
South Carolina House of Representatives journal, 20 January 1790 (State Records of South Carolina, Journals of the House of Representatives, 1789-1790), often called the Moors Sundry Act though no Act was passed. Recognizes Moorish nationals as distinct from "Negroes." Sundry Free Moors petition against the SC Negro Act of 1740, asking to be tried "not under the Negro Act", and the House agrees with its committee that they "are not triable by the Law for the better Ordering and Governing of Negroes and other Slaves." The corpus reads the report as confirming: the Heirs are PRINCIPAL CREDITORS and "Friends" of the original Republic not statutory aliens, not classified inventory. The Noun (Moor) overrides the Adjective (Negro). The corpus reads Drew Ali as recovering this 123 years later (not stated in the record).

[E-W4-005-01] W4 1790 counter-move TA MILESTONE
HEIR FIREWALL , 1787 Treaty (Five Reasons for Constitutional Inclusion)
CROSS-REF: Every military operation in this window must operate AROUND this instrument. The 1787 Treaty of Peace and Friendship Supreme Law under Art. VI is the firewall. (A5.5) WHY THE TREATY WAS INCLUDED IN THE CONSTITUTION five converging reasons: (1) DIPLOMATIC NECESSITY Morocco was the first nation to recognize American independence (1777); the new Republic needed international legitimacy and could not afford to alienate its first ally. (2) MILITARY REALITY The Sultan had defeated France at Larache (1765), expelled Portugal from Mazagan (1769), and seized the Betsey (1784). The US had no navy. Antagonizing a proven Mediterranean naval power was not an option. (3) COMMERCIAL ACCESS Mediterranean trade routes were Moroccos to grant or deny. Without the Treaty, American merchant shipping had no protection in the Atlantic-Mediterranean corridor. (4) FRENCH PRESSURE France (Senior Lienholder via 1778 Treaty) had its own rivalry with Morocco (Larache defeat). The Treaty establishes a DIRECT US-Morocco relationship that bypasses France reducing French leverage over American foreign relations. (5) PRECEDENT SETTING The Treaty was ratified July 18, 1787, two months BEFORE the Constitution was signed September 17. It was ratified DURING the Convention. Article VI (supreme Law of the Land) was written WITH THIS TREATY already in force. The Framers knew exactly what they were constitutionalizing. The 1790 committee report on the Sundry Free Moors petition is the last recorded acknowledgment of Moorish standing before classification overwrites it. The Root Password functions; the military lane must neutralize the treatys protections indirectly.

[E-W4-010-01] W4 1793 innovation TA MILESTONE
Eli Whitney: cotton gin
Patented 1794. Multiplies cotton processing 50x (one worker cleaned about a pound of fiber in ten hours; two or three with a gin about fifty pounds a day). Revives a declining slave economy: the gin reversed the region's late 18th century economic decline and raised the demand for enslaved labor. The corpus reads that without the gin slavery might have withered economically. The invention EXTENDS the life of the classification by making cotton profitable at scale.

[E-W4-011-01] W4 1800 legal TA MILESTONE
Convention of Mortefontaine Refinanced, not extinguished
Congress had already cancelled the 1778 treaties unilaterally on 7 July 1798; Article II of the Convention leaves them with 'no operation' until the parties can agree, and leaves 'the indemnities mutually due, or claimed' open. The corpus reads that as refinancing rather than extinguishment: (1) the claims are left open rather than discharged, and three years later the United States assumes up to 20 million francs (about $3.75M) of French debts to American citizens as part of the Louisiana bargain; (2) Article II suspends rather than voids; (3) Article 11 of the 1778 Treaty, the mutual guarantee, is not released article by article, though the Convention names the Treaty of Alliance as a whole; (4) San Ildefonso follows the day after, on 1 October 1800, and France's Louisiana title runs from that Spanish cession. The strongest point a skeptic would attack: if the treaties are abrogated, how does France claim standing to sell Louisiana three years later? The answer is that Frances interest in the American Levant was REFINANCED through the Convention into a new instrument, not eliminated. The lien transforms from military guarantee to financial claim and Napoleon monetizes it in 1803.

[E-W4-013-01] W4 1803 records TA MILESTONE
Louisiana Purchase TITLE LAUNDERING
The U.S. buys Louisiana from France, a willing seller, and BARING BROTHERS BANK (London) with HOPE and CO. (Amsterdam) make it payable: France sells them the $11.25M of US six per cent stock at about 86.5 per 100, and with a further discount for early payment realises about 50.35 million francs, roughly $9.44M. Alexander Baring sits at the Paris negotiation table, then sails to Washington to collect the bonds. A BRITISH bank finances an AMERICAN purchase of FRENCH-HELD, SPANISH-MANAGED, MOORISH LAND; Britain and France are at peace when the bargain is struck on 30 April 1803 and at war from 18 May, for the whole of the financing. The corpus reads the bank leg as title laundering.

[E-W4-016-01] W4 1805 military TA
Barbary Wars First (180105)
The 1801 to 1805 war is fought against Tripoli alone, after its Pasha demands more tribute and declares war; Algiers and Tunis take tribute under the 1795 treaties but are not belligerents until 1815 and 1816. Morocco's treaty, ratified and proclaimed 18 July 1787, is the one Barbary agreement the United States never has to buy again, though Morocco is a belligerent for part of 1802 and a Moroccan warship takes an American brig in August 1803, after which the treaty is renewed at Tangier without tribute. The Barbary Wars prove: Moorish nations still possess naval sovereignty, the US must negotiate, and "Negro" cannot apply to treaty-holding sovereigns.

[E-W4-016-02] W4 1805 counter-move TA MILESTONE
First U.S. military engagement with Moorish maritime network
CROSS-REF: The Barbary Wars are the first American wars fought against North African states; every earlier dealing was diplomatic or tributary. Morocco is not a party to the Tripoli war of 1801 to 1805, but it is not exempt from the conflict: it is a belligerent for part of 1802, a Moroccan warship takes an American brig in 1803, and the 1787 treaty is renewed at Tangier in October 1803. Contemporaneous with the 1803 Louisiana Purchase while the US buys Moorish land through intermediaries, it simultaneously fights Moorish naval powers in the Mediterranean. Two fronts of the same jurisdictional conflict.

[E-W4-017-01] W4 1807 information TA MILESTONE
Fichte: Addresses to the German Nation
NEW (W4-007): Johann Gottlieb Fichte delivers 14 lectures in French-occupied Berlin arguing that NATIONAL EDUCATION is the mechanism for rebuilding a conquered people. The state must control what children learn from birth. Individual identity is subordinated to national identity through curriculum. THE FORENSIC KEY: Fichte's Addresses make national education the instrument of national recovery, and the corpus reads his system as the blueprint for compulsory state education. The chain: Fichte (1807), Prussian gymnasium system (1810s), Massachusetts Board of Education (1837), Horace Mann visits Prussia (1843, this window), first state compulsory attendance law (1852, W5), Carlisle Indian School (1879, W5), Rockefeller General Education Board (chartered 1902 to 1903, W5) all states compulsory by 1918 (W5) Reece Committee discovers foundation-controlled curriculum (1954, W6). Technology 13 (Educational/Curricular Reclassification): the same mechanism that converts Prussian children into obedient citizens converts indigenous and classified children into the Adjective. Fichte writes during Napoleons occupation the system is designed for a CONQUERED population. When Mann imports it to America, the conquered population is the Heir community. Tier A (Fichtes lectures are a standard philosophical text).

German Coast Uprising Largest slave revolt in US history
January 810. Louisiana. 200500 participants march from sugar parishes toward New Orleans, waving banners and beating drums. Led by Charles Deslondes reportedly of Haitian origin. The revolt occurs on RECENTLY TITLE-LAUNDERED TERRITORY (Louisiana Purchase, 1803 eight years prior). The classified population on the transferred estate resists the transfer. [E-W4-019-01] W4 1813 military TB
Creek civil war as classification war
The Red Stick faction resists assimilation they refuse to adopt European economic and cultural norms. Fort Mims is the clearest example of the fork operating within a SINGLE nation.

[E-W4-019-02] W4 1813 legal TA MILESTONE
Fort Mims / Creek Red Stick War THE MIDDLEWARE TURNS
August 30. Over 700 Red Stick Creek warriors storm Fort Mims in modern-day Alabama. Roughly 250 of the 400 people inside are killed and at least 100 taken captive, the bloodiest single day of the Creek War. THE STAFFING AGENCY FRAMEWORK: Fort Mims is a CIVIL WAR within the Creek Nation. The Creek had been divided by intermarriage with Scottish Highlanders William McIntosh (Scottish father, Creek mother, cousin of the governor of Georgia) leads the assimilated faction. The Red Sticks refuse assimilation. McIntosh functions exactly like the Jewish financiers under Edward I (W0): a middleware population installed to manage extraction from their own people. The Red Sticks attack on Fort Mims is the Noun attacking the compromised intermediary. Chain forward: McIntosh signs the Treaty of Indian Springs (1825), ceding millions of Creek acres without tribal authorization. The Muscogee National Council sentences the signatories to death, and on 30 April 1825 Menawa's Law Menders burn his house and shoot his body more than fifty times. The Noun eliminates the middleware. Andrew Jackson uses Fort Mims as pretext for scorched-earth campaign seizure of some 22 million acres of Creek land Indian Removal Act (1830, this window). The Fort Mims response is the mechanism that converts military conflict into land transfer.

British troops burn many congressional records and the archives of the Navy, War and Treasury Departments. [E-W4-021-02] W4 1815 records TA MILESTONE
Congress of Vienna
Post-Napoleonic restructuring. Redraws the entire European map. Creates the Concert of Europe, a great-power agreement to manage the European balance in which non-European sovereigns have no seat; the corpus reads that as de-recognition. Baring Brothers, who financed the 1803 Louisiana Purchase, are not parties at Vienna; their post-Napoleonic role is the refinancing of the French reparations debt settled at Aix-la-Chapelle in 1818. The international law framework that enables the entire 19th-century foreclosure of Moorish standing. 1787 Treaty still technically Supreme Law; Congress of Vienna creates the international context in which no one enforces it.

[E-W4-023-02] W4 1816b legal TB MILESTONE
Fort Negro destroyed THE INCITING EVENT
July 27. Apalachicola River, SPANISH FLORIDA. ~300 free Black people and Seminoles occupy a British-built fort abandoned after the War of 1812. Andrew Jackson orders its destruction. US forces fire a heated cannonball into the powder magazine ~270 of ~300 occupants killed in a single explosion. Men, women, children. One of the deadliest acts of government violence before the Civil War. THE FORENSIC SEQUENCE: Fort Negro is on SPANISH TERRITORY. The US military attacks a free community on FOREIGN SOVEREIGN SOIL a jurisdictional violation. The community exists because of Spains 1693 sanctuary policy (W2) and the Fort Mose precedent (W3). They are free under Spanish law. The US destroys them to prevent the settlement from becoming a magnet for escaped classified people the same threat Fort Mose represented. THE THREE-EVENT CHAIN: Fort Negro destroyed (1816) First Seminole War (1817) Adams-Ons Treaty / Florida Purchase (1819). The Managers destroy the free community, then fight the war, then BUY THE JURISDICTION to make it legal retroactively. The Florida Purchase is partly about eliminating the jurisdictional gap that allowed free communities to exist outside US classification. Without Fort Negro, the Seminole Wars entry looks like it starts in a vacuum. With Fort Negro, you see the sequence: destroy first, legalize later. Source: Andrew Jacksons letters to Secretary of War William Crawford (May 1816) explicitly orders destruction, calls it a harbor for runaway slaves and the haunt of the most vicious of the Indian tribes. Papers of Andrew Jackson (Tier B).
sources: [A live_verified] src-adams-onis-treaty-1819 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/8/252

THREE wars spanning 40 years. Andrew Jackson invades Florida to crush the alliance of Black Seminoles and Maroons DIRECTLY following the Fort Negro destruction (1816). The Seminole specifically refuse to surrender Black Seminoles people who exist OUTSIDE the classification system entirely. The Seminole Wars are the military enforcement of the Adjective against a nation that doesnt recognize it. Lane 2 and Lane 3 running together for four decades. THE CLASSIFICATION FORK: The standard narrative calls these Indian wars. But the Black Seminoles who are the primary targets of recapture are classified as Negroes under US law. The same conflict is simultaneously an Indian war (Seminole resistance) and a slave rebellion (Black Seminole refusal of classification). The fork cannot even classify the wars it produces.

[E-W4-026-01] W4 1822 legal TC MILESTONE
Denmark Vesey Plot HAITIAN CONTAGION CONFIRMED
Charleston, South Carolina. Denmark Vesey a FREE Black man organizes thousands to seize the city, liberate the enslaved, and sail to Haiti. Vesey explicitly cites the Haitian Revolution as inspiration. His organizational hub: Emanuel AME Church, Charleston part of the free Black institutional network (AME Church, 1816). Betrayed by informants. THREE FORENSIC CONNECTIONS: (1) Haitian contagion: Vesey proves the Managers fear that Haiti will inspire domestic counter-moves is CONFIRMED. This chains to Haitis 58-year non-recognition (already in this window) the quarantine is strategic. (2) Institutional destruction: Response 35 executed, Emanuel AME Church DEMOLISHED. The Managers destroy the Nouns organizational infrastructure. This chains to the free Black institutional network entry (this window) Veseys plot shows WHY the Managers target those institutions. (3) Classification hardening against FREE Nouns: South Carolinas Seamen Acts (1822) any free Black sailor entering Charleston harbor is jailed until their ship departs. The classification hardens against free Nouns specifically because Vesey was free. THE FORK: Vesey was free. He used institutional networks (the Church). He cited a foreign revolutionary precedent (Haiti). Under the forensic reading, this is a sovereign coordinating a military operation using institutional infrastructure and international precedent. Under the Adjective, it is a slave conspiracy. Source: Trial records, SC Department of Archives and History. Lionel Kennedy Thomas Parker, An Official Report of the Trials of Sundry Negroes (1822) (Tier B).

[E-W4-027-02] W4 1823 financial TA
Johnson v. McIntosh Discovery Doctrine installed
Chief Justice Marshall: private citizens cannot purchase land from Native Americans. Only the federal government holds "right of preemption." Installs the discovery doctrine in US law; the Vatican's 2023 statement records that scholars trace the doctrine's basis to the bulls Dum Diversas (1452), Romanus Pontifex (1455) and Inter Caetera (1493). The Heirs Allodial title reduced to "right of occupancy." Vatican repudiates this doctrine in 2023 200 years later.

[E-W4-031-01] W4 1831 military TA
Nat Turners Rebellion
August. Southampton County, Virginia. 55 to 58 white people killed. Response: about 120 Black people killed in reprisals, most uninvolved, with some studies putting the figure higher. Virginia legislature debates ABOLISHING SLAVERY entirely in 183132 and votes it down. The closest the classification comes to being legislated away before the Civil War. The vote fails along the line between the enslaving east and the western counties, on property-right and public-opinion arguments; the corpus reads the cotton economy as the reason underneath.

[E-W4-031-02] W4 1831 legal TA MILESTONE
Most intense legal restriction wave in antebellum period
Nearly every slave state responds: literacy laws (all but Maryland, Kentucky and Tennessee criminalize teaching enslaved people to read), assembly restrictions (no gatherings without white supervision), preaching restrictions (Black preachers banned or monitored), free Black mobility and livelihood curtailed (settlement barred in Alabama; trades closed in Virginia to those refusing removal to Liberia), manumission tightened (not yet sourced). The classification HARDENS in response to resistance. Turner proves the Adjective requires constant legal reinforcement. Lanes 2, 3, 5, and 8 all fire simultaneously.

[E-W4-032-01] W4 1831 military TA MILESTONE
Cherokee Nation v. Georgia
Marshall rules the Cherokee are a 'domestic dependent nation': a State, and uniformly treated as one since the settlement of the country, but NOT a foreign state in the sense the Constitution uses that term, and therefore without standing to sue Georgia in this Court. The syllabus affirms both halves: 'The acts of our Government plainly recognise the Cherokee Nation as a State, and the Courts are bound by those acts.' Creates a new jurisdictional category: the 'ward' of the federal government, in 'a state of pupilage'. The Heir is not stripped of statehood; the Heir is stripped of the forum in which statehood could be asserted.
sources: [A live_verified] src-cherokee-nation-1831 https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/30/1/

[E-W4-034-01] W4 1832 legal TA MILESTONE
Worcester v. Georgia
Marshall rules Cherokee Nation is sovereign territory where Georgia law has no force: 'a distinct community occupying its own territory, with boundaries accurately described, in which the laws of Georgia can have no force, and which the citizens of Georgia have no right to enter'. The ruling is not enforced: the Georgia court refuses to release Worcester, Governor Lumpkin promises 'the most prompt and determined resistance', the missionaries stay in prison until January 1833, and Jackson writes to John Coffee on 7 April 1832 that the decision 'has fell still born, and they find that it cannot coerce Georgia to yield to its mandate'. THE LINE LONG ATTRIBUTED TO JACKSON, that Marshall has made his decision and may now enforce it, IS APOCRYPHAL: it is not in the judgment, it is sourced nowhere here, and the scholarship treats it as invention. What is documented is the non-enforcement itself, which the corpus sources where it actually happened, in Scott's 1838 removal address. The Software exists; the Hardware (military force) overrides it.
sources: [A live_verified] src-worcester-georgia-1832 https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/31/515/; [A live_verified] src-w4cc-jackson-to-brigadier-general-john-coffee-washi-8974 https://archive.org/download/correspondenceof0004unse_u3y7/correspondenceof0004unse_u3y7_djvu.txt; [B live_verified] src-w4cc-worcester-v-georgia-wikipedia-enforcement-sect-80c8 https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worcester_v._Georgia

threads: T-DISCOVERY-DOCTRINE

[E-W4-035-01] W4 1832 information TC MILESTONE
Rafinesque: Primitive Black Nations of America
NEW (W4-010): Professor Constantine Samuel Rafinesque publishes 'The Primitive Black Nations of America' in Atlantic Journal and Friend of Knowledge. The piece is a notice of an award, not the research: the Society of Geography in Paris awarded a gold medal of 100 francs for two Memoirs, communicated to him by Warden, former US Consul in Paris, and Jomard of the Institute, and Rafinesque states that the details are 'given at length, proved by authorities and compared Vocabularies, in my Memoir'. That Memoir is not held by this corpus. What the article carries is an enumeration of twelve black nations he claims to trace in North and South America, and his own language is contemptuous throughout: item 4 gives 'the black Aroras of Raleigh, or Faruras of the Spaniards, ugly black or brown Negroes, yet existing near the Oronoco, and language known, called Monkeys by their neighbours'; item 7 gives 'the Nigritas of Martyr in Darien, yet existing in Choco under the name of Chuanas or Gannas or Chinos... Ugly black or red Negroes'; item 10 gives 'the Enslen or Esteros of New California, ugly blackish Negroes'. THE FORENSIC KEY: a European scientist, rewarded by a Paris learned society, is enumerating melanated aboriginal populations in print at the same moment the U.S. is spending three wars and $40 million (Seminole Wars, running concurrently) to reclassify and destroy them. The science records the Noun in the same breath that it degrades it; the military enforces the Adjective. They run simultaneously. The military wins.
sources: [A live_verified] src-rafinesque-atlantic-journal-1832 https://archive.org/details/atlanticjournal1rafi
links: rafinesque-the-american-nations-1836, linnaeus-systema-1758

[E-W4-036-01] W4 1836 population TA MILESTONE
Rafinesque: The American Nations
NEW (W4-011): Rafinesques magnum opus. Full synthesis documenting melanated aboriginal nations, linguistic connections across hemispheres, and identification of Yahuwah worship among pre-Columbian populations. Connects Yamasee to broader hemispheric civilizational network. Published four years after Rafinesque's 1832 Atlantic Journal notice that the Society of Geography in Paris had awarded him a gold medal of 100 francs for two Memoirs, and two years before the Cherokee removal of 1838 to 1839, in the year the Senate ratified the Treaty of New Echota that compelled it. The scientist documenting who these people ARE publishes while the military relocates them. Science and violence operating on the same population simultaneously. Source: Rafinesque, The American Nations (1836). Tier B.

[E-W4-037-01] W4 1832 records TA MILESTONE
Jackson kills the Second Bank
Andrew Jackson vetoes recharter of the Second Bank of the United States. Deposits moved to state banks (the 'pet banks') in 1833. The Collection Engine is temporarily disrupted; the Bank War is one strand in the causation of the Panic of 1837, alongside the Specie Circular of 1836 and the Bank of England's rate rise, and the panic collapses the western land bubble. The Managers fight over WHO controls the siphon, not WHETHER to siphon.
