[E-W4-038-01] W4 1837 population TB MILESTONE
Horace Mann Massachusetts Board of Education
NEW (W4-008): Mann appointed first Secretary of the Massachusetts Board of Education. In 1843 he visits PRUSSIA specifically to study the Fichte/gymnasium model (1807, this window). Returns and implements: age-graded classrooms, standardized curriculum, professional teacher training, compulsory attendance. Mann explicitly argues education must produce citizens who serve the state. THE FORENSIC KEY: Mann imports a system designed for a conquered population (Fichte wrote for Prussians under Napoleonic occupation) and deploys it in a republic where 14% of the population is classified as property and another ~5% is under active military removal. The standardized curriculum Mann creates will be the SAME curriculum into which Brown v. Board (1954, W6) integrates the classified population. The Fichte Mann Carlisle chain: the education technology crosses from European military context to American civilian context to boarding school deployment against indigenous children. Technology 13 operational. Chain forward: Massachusetts passes first compulsory attendance law 1852 (W5 opening year). Tier A (Manns annual reports are standard educational history).
sources: [A citation_only] src-mann-prussian-report-1843
links: fichte-addresses-to-the-1807, before-after-photos-as-propaganda-1879

Five Civilized Tribes removed
Cherokee, Choctaw (~1831, 2,500 die), Creek (~1836), Chickasaw (~1837), Seminole (resist until 1842). ~60,000+ people relocated. The population is processed westward to clear the land for the expanding Cotton Kingdom.

Trail of Tears
Cherokee forced removal. ~16,000 people marched 1,200 miles. 4,0008,000 die en route. Removal executed by federal troops despite Worcester v. Georgia ruling. The legal framework says one thing; the military does another. Violence overrides law.

Cotton production expands massively

[E-W4-040-01] W4 1841 legal TA MILESTONE
Amistad ruling TREATY LAW OVERRIDES CLASSIFICATION
Supreme Court rules the Mende captives are FREE because they were illegally enslaved under international treaty (the Anglo-Spanish treaty banning the slave trade). The Noun wins in a US court USING TREATY LAW the exact mechanism available via the 1787 Morocco Treaty. The Amistad is the PRECEDENT that proves treaty-based standing can override the classification in a US courtroom. The Court holds the Mende 'were never the lawful slaves of Ruiz, or Montez', having been kidnapped and carried to Cuba 'in violation of the laws and treaties of Spain'. John Quincy Adams, arguing for them, expressly declined to rest on treaty law: 'no law, statute or constitution, no code, no treaty' reached the case, he told the Court, pointing to the Declaration of Independence. If treaty law can void the classification for the Mende, it can void it for the Moors. The absence of this case from the Moorish sovereignty narrative is itself a data point the precedent exists, but awareness of its applicability has been suppressed.
sources: [A live_verified] src-amistad-1841 https://supreme.justia.com/cases/federal/us/40/518/; [A live_verified] src-w4cc-argument-of-john-quincy-adams-1841-same-passag-829d https://archive.org/download/argumentofjohnqu1841adam/argumentofjohnqu1841adam_djvu.txt; [A live_verified] src-w4cc-united-states-v-the-amistad-40-u-s-518-library-ca8e https://tile.loc.gov/storage-services/service/ll/usrep/usrep040/usrep040518/usrep040518.pdf
links: creole-mutiny-1841, moors-sundry-act-1790, somerset-v-stewart-1772
threads: T-SOFTWARE-NEUTRALITY

[E-W4-041-01] W4 1841b legal TA MILESTONE
Creole Mutiny JURISDICTIONAL NAVIGATION AT SEA
November. Enslaved people aboard the American brig Creole revolt, seize control of the ship, and sail to Nassau, British Bahamas. Britain, having abolished slavery in 1833, frees those not implicated in the rising at once; the Admiralty Court at Nassau releases the last seventeen on 16 April 1842, and 128 of the 135 aboard end up free. The US demands their return. Britain refuses. Described by the Smithsonian and others as the most successful revolt of enslaved people in US history. TWO MARITIME REVOLTS IN THE SAME YEAR: Amistad and Creole together prove the pattern the classification system breaks at MARITIME JURISDICTIONAL SEAMS. Amistad navigates US/Spanish treaty law. Creole navigates US/British jurisdictional boundaries. The Noun escapes the Adjective by crossing legal boundaries at sea. THE MATRYOSHKA FRAMEWORK: Same logic as the Fort Mose/Spanish Florida sanctuary (W2/W3) but on water competing Managerial jurisdictions with different classification rules. The classified population navigates between them. THE CREOLE FREED MORE PEOPLE THAN THE AMISTAD (128 vs. 53) but is far less known itself a Lane 8 observation. The Amistad became a Supreme Court case and a Hollywood film. The Creole was settled by international arbitration under the claims convention of 8 February 1853 (the umpire's award of $110,330 on 9 January 1855 went to the claimants, the owners) and is largely absent from the standard narrative. Source: FRUS (Secretary of State Daniel Webster / Lord Ashburton diplomatic correspondence). Senate records. 1853 Claims Convention arbitration (Tier B).
sources: [A live_verified] src-riaa-creole-bates-award-1854 https://legal.un.org/riaa/cases/vol_xxix/26-53.pdf; [B live_verified] src-w4cc-j-b-moore-history-and-digest-of-the-internatio-72ab https://archive.org/download/historyanddiges09moorgoog/historyanddiges09moorgoog_djvu.txt; [B live_verified] src-w4cc-smithsonian-magazine-7-november-2017-8b1b https://www.smithsonianmag.com/smart-news/slave-revolt-ended-128-enslaved-people-free-bahamas-180967070/; [B live_verified] src-w4cc-blackpast-org-the-creole-case-1841-aa28 https://blackpast.org/african-american-history/creole-case-1841/; [B live_verified] src-w4cc-legislation-gov-uk-the-national-archives-uk-ca-8c64 https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/1833/73/enacted/data.htm; [B live_verified] src-w4cc-creole-mutiny-wikipedia-b806 https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creole_case; [A live_verified] src-w4cc-united-states-v-the-amistad-40-u-s-518-library-ca8e https://tile.loc.gov/storage-services/service/ll/usrep/usrep040/usrep040518/usrep040518.pdf; [B live_verified] src-w4cc-j-b-moore-history-and-digest-of-the-internatio-ee50 https://archive.org/download/historyanddiges10moorgoog/historyanddiges10moorgoog_djvu.txt
links: amistad-1841, somerset-v-stewart-1772, maroon-communities-forming-counter-move-1655

[E-W4-042-01] W4 1845 military TB MILESTONE
Hudson: Mauritanian Races Speech
NEW (W4-013): Congressional Globe, 28th Congress, 2nd Session. Congressman Charles Hudson, on the official record, one year before the Mexican-American War, explicitly identifies the populations west of the Nueces/Rio Bravo as Mauritanian and Moorish, acknowledges that Mexico properly belongs to them, and predicts that if the races cross the boundary, one or the other race must be conquered, if not extinguished. THE FORENSIC KEY: A sitting U.S. congressman NAMES the populations the military is about to seize territory from as MOORS. This is not a retrospective analysis it is a contemporary identification on the official legislative record. Hudson connects the Seminole Wars (eastern front) to the upcoming Mexican-American War (western front) as two flanks of the same campaign against the same civilizational population. The stupendous deserts between the Nueces and the Bravo rivers are the boundary between the Anglo-Saxon and the Mauritanian races. The Managers KNOW who they are displacing. Source: Congressional Globe (Tier A official legislative record). Cross-reference: Rafinesque (1832, this window), Seminole Wars (this window), Mexican-American War (below), Disturnell Map (1848, below).
sources: [A live_verified] src-cong-globe-ingersoll-moorish-1845 https://archive.org/details/sim_united-states-congress-congressional-globe_1845-01-06_14_6

[E-W4-044-01] W4 1848 legal TA MILESTONE
Treaty of Guadalupe Hidalgo + Disturnell Map
Mexico cedes more than 525,000 sq miles: California, Nevada, Utah, Arizona, New Mexico, parts of Colorado/Wyoming. US pays $15M, the same nominal price as the Louisiana Purchase, plus the assumption of American claims against Mexico. The entire Southwest transferred. Spanish and Mexican land grants overwritten: the Senate struck Article X, which had guaranteed them, and the 1851 Act put the burden of proof on the grantees at a cost that was 'often the land itself'. THE DISTURNELL MAP (W4-014): The official map used during negotiations is J. Disturnells Mapa de los Estados Unidos de Mjico (1847). This map labels Antigua Residencia de los Aztecas (Ancient Residence of the Aztecs) in the northern region near modern Utah/Arizona INSIDE the territory being transferred. The U.S. government negotiates its largest territorial seizure while looking at a map that acknowledges the aboriginal homeland is inside the territory being seized. CARTOGRAPHIC CONFESSION on a treaty document. Hudsons 1845 speech (above) names the same populations as Mauritanian. The Disturnell Map labels their homeland. The treaty transfers both. Neither the populations nor their title are consulted. Source: Library of Congress (Tier A).

Gold discovered at Sutters Mill, Jan 24. Within a year, 300,000 arrive. The resource that drives the land seizure. Californias gold finances the next phase of extraction.

[E-W4-046-01] W4 1850 legal TA MILESTONE
Fugitive Slave Act strengthened
Federal marshals required to capture escaped classified people in ANY state. Penalties for helping. Classification has federal enforcement teeth across entire Corporation. 1851: Christiana Resistance (PA) armed Black farmers resist federal marshals, the slaveholder Edward Gorsuch killed, 38 local men charged with treason; the first defendant is acquitted in about fifteen minutes and the rest are released. 1854: Anthony Burns (Boston) fugitive returned under military escort through streets of Boston, over 50,000 line the route in protest; the government's cost is estimated at $40,000, and as high as $100,000 by James M. McPherson, to return one man. Military occupation of Northern cities to enforce a Southern classification.

California free; Utah/New Mexico territories open to popular sovereignty on slavery; Fugitive Slave Act strengthened; DC slave trade abolished (not slavery). Every "compromise" is an internal negotiation among Managers about how to manage the classification.

[E-W4-048-01] W4 1854 military TA
Kansas-Nebraska Act
Repeals Missouri Compromise (1820). Opens Kansas and Nebraska to slavery by popular sovereignty, reopening the question in the western territories. "Popular sovereignty" = the classification can now expand without limit. The pre-Civil War crisis accelerates.

[E-W4-048-02] W4 1854 legal TA MILESTONE
Kansas-Nebraska Act / Orphan Trains begin
Kansas-Nebraska opens northern territories to slavery by popular sovereignty. The year after Charles Loring Brace founds the Children's Aid Society (1853), the Orphan Trains begin: the first large expedition leaves in September 1854 and the first group of 45 children arrives at Dowagiac, Michigan, on 1 October 1854. The population processing pipelines multiply: classified labor pushed south and west; displaced children pushed west on trains. Window 5 begins.

[E-W4-049-01] W4 1816 financial TA
Second Bank of the United States chartered - the government takes one fifth
April 10, 1816. Congress incorporated the subscribers to the Bank of the United States at 3 Stat. 266, chapter XLIV, with a capital of thirty-five million dollars in 350,000 shares of one hundred dollars. Seventy thousand of those shares, seven million dollars, were subscribed and paid for by the United States itself. The remaining 280,000 were subscribed by individuals, companies and corporations. The charter expired and the Act of March 3, 1819 amended it.
sources: [A live_verified] src-second-bank-charter-1816 https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/STATUTE-3/pdf/STATUTE-3-Pg266.pdf

[E-W4-050-01] W4 1788 legal TA
United States Constitution ratified
1788. The Constitution drafted at Philadelphia in September 1787 was ratified by the ninth state, New Hampshire, in June 1788, satisfying Article VII's condition that ratification by nine states suffice to establish it between the states so ratifying. The new government commenced the following year. This entry exists because two W3 dossiers reference the ratification by event_id and could not resolve it, one of them the Morocco treaty dossier, for which the treaty's pre-constitutional date is the operative point.

[E-W4-051-01] W4 1824 legal TA
Vagrancy Act 1824 - idleness as an offence
1824. The Vagrancy Act of 5 Geo. 4 c. 83 consolidated English law on rogues, vagabonds and idle and disorderly persons, making want of visible means of subsistence, coupled with wandering abroad, lodging out and failure to give a good account of oneself, a punishable condition rather than a discrete act. This entry exists because the W0 dossier on the Statute of Labourers and the W3 dossier on transport contracts both reference it by event_id as the continuation of status-based labour compulsion and could not resolve it.
sources: [A live_verified] src-vagrancy-act-1824 https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/Geo4/5/83/section/4; [A live_verified] src-w4cc-vagrancy-act-1824-section-iv-as-enacted-legisl-9647 https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/Geo4/5/83/enacted/data.htm

[E-W4-052-01] W4 1851 land TA
California Land Act - burden of proof relocated onto the grant holder
3 March 1851, 9 Stat. 631. Establishes a commission. Every person claiming under a Mexican grant must present the claim. Burden of presentation and of proof on the holder. The act does not confiscate. It relocates the obligation, and the cost and delay of discharging it fall on the claimant. Section 13 deems claims not presented within two years 'part of the public domain of the United States'.
sources: [A live_verified] src-us-9-631-california-land-1851 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/9/631; [A live_verified] src-w4cc-united-states-statutes-at-large-volume-9-ch-xl-bc5b https://archive.org/download/us_stat_9/c31_djvu.txt

[E-W4-053-01] W4 1846 financial TA
Importation Act 1846 - the Corn Laws repealed
26 June 1846, 9 and 10 Vict. c. 22. Substitutes a reduced sliding scale of duties on imported corn until 1 February 1849 and a nominal fixed duty of one shilling a quarter thereafter; its one repeal clause (section V) repeals the prohibition on importing ground corn. 'Repeal of the Corn Laws' is the conventional name for the phase-out, not a description of the enacting words. Carried here as a placed but unanalysed node, in the same season Parliament is told the Irish crop failure will continue.
sources: [A live_verified] src-uk-9-10-vict-c22-corn-1846 https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/Vict/9-10/22/pdfs/ukpga_18460022_en.pdf; [A live_verified] src-hansard-hc-1846-02-20-potato-disease https://api.parliament.uk/historic-hansard/commons/1846/feb/20/the-potato-disease

[E-W4-054-01] W4 1847 legal TA MILESTONE
Irish Poor Law Extension - a class defined out of destitute
8 June 1847, 10 and 11 Vict. c. 31. Occupiers of more than a quarter of an acre are put outside the class the relieving body may assist. DEFINITIONAL EXCLUSION. The statute never says a person shall starve. It defines who counts as destitute and forbids the Board of Guardians to relieve an occupier of more than a quarter acre, in or out of the workhouse; the relieving officer's provisional relief in sudden and urgent necessity runs only to the Guardians' next meeting. The relief is not refused. The applicant is defined out of the category that receives it.
sources: [A live_verified] src-uk-10-11-vict-c31-irish-poor-1847 https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/Vict/10-11/31/pdfs/ukpga_18470031_en.pdf

[E-W4-055-01] W4 1846 information TA
February 1846 - the second season's failure stated in Parliament
HC Deb, 20 February 1846. The continuation of the potato failure into the coming season is stated in the House. SIX MONTHS BEFORE the second failure is reported to the same House, on 17 August 1846. The foreknowledge is on the record, in the chamber, in February. What follows cannot be described as unforeseen by the body that legislated the relief regime the following year.
sources: [A live_verified] src-hansard-hc-1846-02-20-potato-disease https://api.parliament.uk/historic-hansard/commons/1846/feb/20/the-potato-disease; [A live_verified] src-w4cc-distress-in-ireland-hc-deb-17-august-1846-lord-0c38 https://api.parliament.uk/historic-hansard/commons/1846/aug/17/distress-in-ireland-measures-of-relief

[E-W4-056-01] W4 1849 sovereign TA
A guardianship over a minor sovereign converts into annexation
HC Deb, 24 April 1849. A protectorate entered by treaty AT THE LOCAL NOBILITY'S REQUEST (Bhyrowal, 16 December 1846), over a sovereign who is a minor, converts into annexation: on 29 March 1849, four weeks before this debate, Dulleep Sing resigns all right, title and claim to the sovereignty of the Punjab. The instrument that begins as custody ends as title. The request supplies the legitimacy at the front end and is not required again at the back end.
sources: [A live_verified] src-hansard-hc-1849-04-24-punjab https://api.parliament.uk/historic-hansard/commons/1849/apr/24/military-operations-in-the-punjab-vote; [A live_verified] src-w4cc-aitchison-treaties-engagements-and-sanads-vol-9c92 https://archive.org/download/in.ernet.dli.2015.206801/2015.206801.A-Collection_djvu.txt

[E-W4-057-01] W4 1848 population TA
The post-abolition indenture pipeline, described as funded state policy
HC Deb, 19 June 1848. The movement of indentured labour into the sugar colonies is described in Parliament as FUNDED STATE POLICY, and complained of on the floor as underfunded: Sir John Pakington asks why the Act of 1846 was not accompanied 'with some provision for supplying free labour to the colonies', and Mr K. Seymer says the planters were promised 'assistance to promote immigration' and were not given it. Ten years after the apprenticeship ended, the replacement supply is not a private trade the state tolerates. It is a programme the state pays for, and the objection on the floor is that it pays too little.
sources: [A live_verified] src-hansard-hc-1848-06-19-sugar-duties https://api.parliament.uk/historic-hansard/commons/1848/jun/19/sugar-duties; [A live_verified] src-us-12-340-coolie-trade-1862 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/12/340; [A live_verified] src-w4cc-slavery-abolition-act-1833-sections-v-and-vi-t-d712 https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/Will4/3-4/73/pdfs/ukpga_18330073_en.pdf

[E-W4-058-01] W4 1845 corporate TA
A colony conducted as a negotiation with a chartered company
HC Deb, 4 August 1845. The colony's affairs are conducted as a NEGOTIATION BETWEEN THE COLONIAL OFFICE AND A CHARTERED COMPANY, in the year the war begins. The settler legislature that will pass the Settlements Act eighteen years later does not yet exist. What exists is a company and a department dividing the question between them.

[E-W4-059-01] W4 1848 information TA
Marker 9 is not a conflict, and the contested number is audited in public
HL Deb 10 April and HC Deb 13 April 1848. The responsible minister describes the Kennington Common demonstration ON THE DAY, and it is not a conflict. Three days later the disputed signature count is audited in public with method, duration and result stated, and the claimant concedes in part. A dispute over a number, settled by showing the working.

[E-W4-060-01] W4 1849 sovereign TA
Kingdom or province, and the House's laughter records which reading won
HC Deb, 21 July 1849. Whether Hungary is a kingdom or a province is argued in the Commons while one great power's army is in the field on the other side of the question. THE LAUGHTER IS IN THE RECORD, and it registers which reading the House found serious. Standing is contested in vocabulary while it is being settled by force elsewhere.

HC Deb, 7 May 1850. Voluntary funding for the Exhibition, and a DESIGN-PIRACY QUESTION RAISED A YEAR BEFORE IT OPENED. The problem of who owns a displayed design is anticipated before there is a display. The intellectual-property question arrives ahead of the exhibit that would create it.

[E-W4-062-01] W4 1853 information TA
A dynastic rupture treated in the House as forty years of continuity
HC Deb, 18 February 1853. The dynastic change of December 1852 is answered in the House, ten weeks later, with CONTINUITY OF A FORTY-YEAR PEACE, against a widely held expectation of rupture that Disraeli sets out in order to argue it down. What the corpus can show here is not what happened in Paris but how quickly it was absorbed into an unbroken story in London.

[E-W4-063-01] W4 1836 legal TA MILESTONE
Treaty of Peace and Friendship, United States and Morocco, 16 September 1836: the instrument the ICJ actually construed
Signed 16 September 1836, 8 Stat. 484, twenty five articles. Article 25 verbatim: the treaty runs fifty years, after which it "shall continue to be binding on both parties, until the one shall give twelve months notice to the other of an intention to abandon it". It was still operative law in 1952, when the International Court of Justice applied it; no notice of abandonment is recorded. Not one of the twenty five articles mentions the treaty of 1787: the cross-reference on the page is the Statutes at Large editor's.
sources: [A live_verified] src-treaty-morocco-1836 https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/STATUTE-8/pdf/STATUTE-8-Pg484.pdf; [A live_verified] src-icj-morocco-1952 https://www.icj-cij.org/case/11
links: heir-firewall-1787-1790, w3-1787-legal, icj-rules-on-1787-treaty-1952
threads: T-SOFTWARE-NEUTRALITY

[E-W4-063-02] W4 1836 records TA
The official printing cross-references the two instruments: a footnote at 8 Stat. 484 points to the treaty of January 1787 at 8 Stat. 100
framework: The sovereignty instrument that survives the protectorate. Article 25's indefinite-force clause is why a United States treaty with Morocco was still operative law in 1952, which is the twentieth-century hinge the corpus had been attributing to the 1787 treaty
assessment: Weight: Proven (the instrument read in full from the Statutes at Large, Article 25 and the cross-reference footnote verbatim) | Warrant: Closed (the treaty text) · Open (whether the two W6 titles are amended, which is a separate frozen-field ruling)

[E-W5-007-03] W5 1861 legal TC
Sine Die Adjournment - CONSTITUTIONAL BREAKPOINT
March 27-28. Seven Southern states walk out of the 36th Congress. Senate adjourns “sine die” - “without day.” No quorum to set reconvening date. Lincoln convenes both Houses July 4 under Article II “extraordinary Occasions.” THE DE JURE REPUBLIC DISSOLVES. Everything after 1861 operates under extraordinary/emergency authority. The Congress that reconvenes is a wartime Congress under executive command, not the original constitutional body. The Corporate Shift begins here.
sources: [A live_verified] src-muscatine-journal-sine-die-motion-1861 http://www.loc.gov/resource/sn84027253/1861-03-29/ed-1/?sp=2
links: dred-scott-press-coverage-1857, 160-acres-free-homestead-1862, 13th-amendment-1865

[E-W5-011-01] W5 1865 financial TA
Freedman's Savings and Trust Company incorporated, its board named in the statute
Chap. XCII, 13 Stat. 510, approved 3 March 1865. The bank chartered to hold the deposits of freedpeople was incorporated by a board of fifty white trustees named on the face of the instrument, drawn from New York, Massachusetts, Rhode Island, Pennsylvania and Ohio, among them Peter Cooper, William C. Bryant, Gerritt Smith and John Jay. Distinct from the Freedmen's Bureau at 13 Stat. 507, a different instrument passed the same day.

[E-W5-011-02] W5 1865 legal TA
13th Amendment
Bureau of Refugees, Freedmen, and Abandoned Lands (1865-1872). Creates the largest body of federal records documenting the lives of formerly enslaved people, more than one thousand linear feet of field office records alone. Marriage records, indentures of apprenticeship, labor contracts, complaint registers, records of abandoned and confiscated land, hospital registers. The corpus reads it as the single largest counter-archive to Plecker’s 1924 overwrite. FORENSIC QUESTION: what survived? The Bureau was a War Department agency from its creation; when it was abolished effective 30 June 1872 its remaining work and records passed to the Freedmen's Branch of the Adjutant General's Office, and the records are now RG 105 at the National Archives.
sources: [A live_verified] src-13th-amendment-1865 https://www.archives.gov/founding-docs/amendments-11-27; [B live_verified] src-w5cc-records-of-the-bureau-of-refugees-freedmen-and-5a09 https://www.archives.gov/research/guide-fed-records/groups/105.html; [B live_verified] src-w5cc-reginald-washington-the-freedmen-s-bureau-pres-2492 https://www.archives.gov/publications/prologue/2002/summer/freedmens-bureau.html; [A live_verified] src-us-13-507-freedmens-bureau-1865 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/13/507; [A live_verified] src-freedmens-bureau-discontinued-1872 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/17/366

[E-W5-016-04] W5 1871 legal TB
DC Organic Act: the District constituted a body corporate
41st Congress, Session III, Chapter 62, p. 419. Constitutes the District 'a body corporate for municipal purposes' (16 Stat. 419). The word 'private' is not in the statute; reading this incorporation as the Republic becoming a Corporation is the corpus's inference. Sine Die (1861) dissolved the de jure Republic; the Organic Act (1871) installs the Corporation. Ten years between death of one entity and birth of another. “Constitution OF” replaces “Constitution FOR.” The 14th Amendment “citizens” (1868) are now subjects of a corporation, not a republic.
sources: [B live_verified] src-dc-organic-act-1871 https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Page:United_States_Statutes_at_Large_Volume_16.djvu/453
links: 400-patents-held-by-black-inventors-1900, oklahoma-statehood-1907, garrett-morgan-traffic-signal-patent-1923
threads: T-FRANCHISE-TRANSFER

[E-W5-017-02] W5 1872 legal TA
Freedmen's Bureau discontinued by a proviso inside an appropriations act
17 Stat. 366. The Bureau was not ended by a dedicated statute. It was discontinued by a proviso inside "An Act making Appropriations for sundry civil Expenses of the Government": "Provided, That the Bureau of Refugees, Freedmen, and Abandoned Lands shall be discontinued from and after June thirtieth, eighteen hundred and seventy-two". The clause sits under a line item for the bounty and prize-money claims of colored soldiers and sailors.
sources: [A live_verified] src-freedmens-bureau-discontinued-1872 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/17/366

[E-W5-017-04] W5 1872 innovation TA
Elijah McCoy: automatic lubrication
US Patent 129,843. The automatic lubricator became standard equipment on long-distance locomotives, and was used on transatlantic ships and in factory machines. Often credited with putting "the real McCoy" into the language, though etymologists trace the phrase to the Scots "the real MacKay", recorded in 1856. By 1900 the Patent Office's Henry E. Baker had compiled a list of around 370 Black inventors, nearly 400 patents; by 1913 he had verified about 800

[E-W5-022-01] W5 1877 legal TB
Hayes-Tilden Compromise
Disputed 1876 election resolved by Electoral Commission (19 Stat. 227). Hayes gets presidency; Reconstruction ENDS. Federal troops withdrawn from the South. The classified population is abandoned. The deal: the Managers trade enforcement of the 14th/15th Amendments for control of the executive. The “protection” was always contingent on political convenience.
sources: [B live_verified] src-electoral-commission-act-1877 https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Page:United_States_Statutes_at_Large_Volume_19.djvu/254

[E-W5-023-02] W5 1879 information TB

[E-W5-026-02] W5 1884 information TA
Charlotte Astor: 1,113 children sent
CAS annual report documents Mrs. John Jacob Astor III sponsoring the placing-out of 1,113 homeless children 'during the past few years, at an expense of $16,657', her ledger line headed EMIGRATION. At least one contemporary notice framed it as philanthropy, calling her 'this noble, philanthropic woman' while quoting the New-York Tribune on 'the transfer of the children to new surroundings' (The Monitor, Woodstown N.J., 22 Feb 1884); a Louisiana paper reported the figures without comment. The corpus reads it as population transfer.
sources: [A live_verified] src-cas-annual-report-32-1884 https://archive.org/details/annualreportofch3238chil; [B live_verified] src-monitor-woodstown-1884-02-22 http://www.loc.gov/resource/sn91064052/1884-02-22/ed-1/?sp=1; [B live_verified] src-donaldsonville-chief-1884-01-19 http://www.loc.gov/resource/sn85034248/1884-01-19/ed-1/?sp=1

[E-W5-027-03] W5 1887 legal TB
Dawes Act - Allotment + Classification
86M Native acres taken. But the ACT is only half the weapon. The DAWES ROLLS (enrollment lists, 1898-1914) are a classification engine IDENTICAL to Plecker’s. The Dawes Commission decides blood quantum, assigns identity, determines land access. People who refuse to enroll LOSE their land. People who enroll get allotments that are then swindled via Burke Act (1906) “competency” determinations.
sources: [B live_verified] src-dawes-act-1887 https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Dawes_Act; [B live_verified] src-dawes-commission-act-1893 https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Page:United_States_Statutes_at_Large_Volume_27.djvu/671; [B live_verified] src-curtis-act-1898 https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Page:United_States_Statutes_at_Large_Volume_30.djvu/534
links: pan-american-commercial-conference-1889, riis-how-the-other-half-lives-1890, tesla-ac-motor-at-fair-1893, spanish-american-war-dominates-press-1898

[E-W5-028-05] W5 1889 certification TC
Pan-American Commercial Conference
1889-1890. The corpus reads the Conference as evidence that Central and South American states operated under treaty relations and Iberian-derived title. The invitation text sourced here records the convening and its commercial agenda, not that finding. FEAR: if Northern Heirs (“Negroes”) realize connection to Southern Sovereigns, they could form a Continental Trust dwarfing the U.S. Corporation. The Conference proves the 1787 Treaty has hemispheric implications.
sources: [B live_verified] src-evening-bulletin-american-conference-invitation-1889 http://www.loc.gov/resource/sn87060190/1889-10-03/ed-1/?sp=1

[E-W5-029-04] W5 1890 financial TB
Baring Brothers near-failure: the Bank of England and the Rothschilds organise a guarantee
Baring Brothers near-failure 1890 - same family as 1803 Louisiana Purchase. In November 1890 the Bank of England, the Rothschilds and London joint-stock banks raised a guarantee fund of 10,000,000 pounds to carry the firm through losses on its South American commitments (New-York Tribune, 16 Nov 1890). The same house, through Alexander Baring with Hope and Co. of Amsterdam, had financed the Louisiana Purchase bonds in 1803 (the Baring Archive).
sources: [B live_verified] src-nytribune-1890-11-16-baring http://www.loc.gov/resource/sn83030214/1890-11-16/ed-1/?sp=1; [B live_verified] src-baring-brothers-archive https://baringarchive.org.uk/exhibition/the-louisiana-purchase/

[E-W5-035-03] W5 1897 information TB
Record destruction not investigated
Immigration records back to 1855 gone, about forty-two years of them: the New York State Castle Garden series 1855 to 1890, the Barge Office series 1890 to 1891, and the Ellis Island series 1892 to 1897. Ship manifests before June 1897 burned; the Customs Lists survived - press covers fire as building story, not as data loss

[E-W5-047-01] W5 1914 financial TB
NYSE closed 4 months
Closed 31 July 1914, with stock trading resuming in stages from 12 December: the longest closure in the exchange's history. New York was the LAST major exchange to suspend, every other important exchange having closed the same day. WWI triggers capital flight. The financial system literally shuts down while the new Fed takes control

[E-W5-047-02] W5 1914 innovation TB
Madam C.J. Walker, widely called the first female self-made millionaire
Front-page coverage in The Freeman of Indianapolis, September 1913, carries Walker as a national business figure urging Black women into business: 'THE NEGRO WOMAN IN BUSINESS. Mme. C. J. Walker Urges her Sisters to Rise Above the Wash Tub and Cook Kitchen'. The 'first female self-made millionaire' description is the one attached to her in the press and in later biography; IT IS NOT SOURCED HERE, no wealth figure appears in the coverage, and the priority claim is contested in the scholarship on its own terms. Walker's company records at the Indiana Historical Society are the route to settling it. Note also that the coverage is September 1913 and this entry sits at 1914, a one-year displacement handled under the standing placement policy.
sources: [B live_verified] src-freeman-walker-negro-woman-in-business-1913 http://www.loc.gov/resource/sn82016211/1913-09-20/ed-1/?sp=1

[E-W5-050-03] W5 1917 legal TA
Trading with the Enemy Act, whose press provisions did not survive into the code
Act of 6 October 1917, ch. 106, 40 Stat. 411. Section 19 of the original Act reached foreign language publications. That section is not what is codified at 50 U.S.C. 4305, which carries section 5 and remains in force. Subsection 4305(b)(4) now runs the other way, denying the President authority to regulate the import or export of "any information or informational materials". The Act survives; the censorship reach does not.
sources: [B citation_only] src-trading-with-enemy-act-1917 https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Trading_with_the_Enemy_Act; [A live_verified] src-usc-50-4305-trading-with-enemy https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/50/4305

[E-W5-059-01] W5 1927 violence TC
Prentiss
MS
audit trail: TRIAGED 2026-07-29 (Thread 20 W5 triage). No record invented on unverified recall. CLASS: SCHOLARSHIP-OR-PRESS. TARGET: Prentiss, Mississippi, 1927. The entry gives a place and a year and nothing else. Establish what event is meant before sourcing. ROUTE: Chronicling America. NOTE THE ACCESS FINDING already in this corpus: loc.gov returns 403 to the fetch layer, but Chronicling America's bot interstitial auto-resolved in the in-app browser at tier 2 (recorded at E-W5-040-01). That is the working route for every press-reception claim in this window. | ACCESS ROUTE CORRECTED AND VERIFIED 2026-07-29 (Thread 20). The standing note that loc.gov is blocked is imprecise in three ways and all three were tested. FIRST, loc.gov is NOT blocked at the host level: curl reaches it and returns HTTP 200. SECOND, the endpoint this project recorded, chroniclingamerica.loc.gov/search/pages/results/, is RETIRED and returns HTTP 404 with a Library of Congress error page; Chronicling America was folded into the main loc.gov site. The working endpoint is https://www.loc.gov/collections/chronicling-america/?q=SEARCHTERM&fo=json&c=COUNT , which returns real JSON with a results array, a pagination total, and per-page URLs of the form http://www.loc.gov/resource/{lccn}/{date}/ed-1/?sp={page} . THIRD, the block that does exist is Cloudflare and it is rate-based, not absolute: the first scripted request succeeded and every subsequent one returned HTTP 403 with the 'Just a moment...' interstitial. THE IN-APP BROWSER RESOLVES THE CHALLENGE AND WAS CONFIRMED WORKING against that JSON endpoint on this date, which is what the earlier finding at E-W5-040-01 had recorded without the endpoint detail. SO THE ROUTE FOR THIS ENTRY IS: in-app browser, that JSON URL, one query at a time. A DATE-FILTER CAVEAT, unresolved: start_date, end_date and dateFilterType were all IGNORED by the endpoint, and a query bounded to 1864-65 returned pages from 1932 and 1962. Do not trust date bounding until the correct parameter is found, and check the date on every hit. | ROUTE NOW FULLY MAPPED AND MY OWN CAVEAT CORRECTED 2026-07-29 (Thread 20, second pass). Earlier today I recorded that date filtering was ignored by this endpoint. THAT WAS WRONG and the correction matters, because it was the one thing making this route impractical. The failure was Cloudflare returning an interstitial, not the parameter being rejected. THE WORKING PARAMETER IS dates=YYYY/YYYY. Verified through the in-app browser: the query q=%22sand+creek%22 with dates=1864/1865 returned 115 hits and every result checked fell inside the range. THE FULL WORKING PATTERN, confirmed end to end: https://www.loc.gov/collections/chronicling-america/?q=TERM&dates=Y1/Y2&fo=json&c=N , loaded in the IN-APP BROWSER, then parse document.body.innerText as JSON. Each result carries a page-level citable URL of the form http://www.loc.gov/resource/{lccn}/{date}/ed-1/?sp={page} plus a title that names the paper, its city and the issue date, which is enough to cite a specific newspaper page. ONE THING STILL UNRESOLVED, recorded rather than guessed: narrowing by place did not work as constructed. fa=location_state:colorado returned total 0 on a query that returns 115 without it. Either that is the wrong facet key or Chronicling America has no Colorado holdings for those years. RESOLVE THAT BEFORE ATTEMPTING ANY ENTRY WHOSE CLAIM NAMES A SPECIFIC CITY OR PAPER, because a general period hit must not be attached to a claim about a particular city's press. | FALSE POSITIVE CAUGHT AND REJECTED 2026-07-30 (Thread 20). A search for Prentiss, Mississippi in the 1927 press returned only FIVE pages corpus-wide, and the one read was the Montgomery Advertiser of 23 March 1927 at page 10: 'The members of Hendrix circle of Dexter Avenue Methodist church met at the home of M[rs]. J. L. Prentiss, Tuesday afternoon, with Mrs. Oscar Norman'. That is a SOCIETY PAGE and Prentiss there is a woman's surname, not the town. NOTHING ATTACHED. The entry gives only a place name and a year with an empty body, so there is no event description to search on; five corpus-wide hits suggests the digitised press for 1927 will not carry it. This entry needs the author to say what happened at Prentiss before it can be sourced. | RETESTED WITH A NARROW TERM, AND THE RESULT IS SUBSTANTIVE 2026-07-30 (Thread 20). Following the false-absence lesson, this entry was retested with 'Prentiss' bounded to Mississippi and to 1926 through 1928. It returns 19 pages, up from the 5 that produced a society-column false positive earlier. The page read is the CALIFORNIA EAGLE of Los Angeles, a Black newspaper, for 13 July 1928. WHAT IT CARRIES IS NOT VIOLENCE. Quoted: 'J[.] R. Johnson and his wife would inform you that the white Mississippian[s are] prepared to support Negro education a[nd] that the contribution[s] of sou[the]rn whites ha[ve] made Prentiss what it is today[.] Johnson has caused the whit[e] people of [hi]s town to see'. That is PRENTISS INSTITUTE, a Black school in Prentiss, Mississippi, in a story about white philanthropic support for Negro education. THIS IS A FINDING FOR THE AUTHOR, NOT A FAILED SEARCH. This entry sits in the VIOLENCE lane at 1927 with an EMPTY BODY and no description. The digitised press associates Prentiss, Mississippi in exactly these years with an educational institution and with interracial philanthropy, not with a violent event. Either the entry means something the press did not carry, or its lane or year is wrong. NOTHING ATTACHED, because attaching a school story to a violence-lane entry would be the referent error this thread has caught five times. The author needs to say what happened at Prentiss.
links: wall-street-bombing-1920, tulsa-covered-then-erased-1921, garrett-morgan-traffic-signal-patent-1923

[E-W5-062-01] W5 1922 legal TA
Ozawa v. United States - white means Caucasian
November 13, 1922. The Supreme Court held that Takao Ozawa, born in Japan, was ineligible for naturalisation because the statutory phrase 'free white person' means, in the Court's words, 'only a person of what is popularly known as the Caucasian race'. Ozawa was found to be 'clearly of a race which is not Caucasian and therefore belongs entirely outside the zone on the negative side'. The statute construed is the free-white-persons limitation retained in the Naturalization Act of 1870.
sources: [A live_verified] src-ozawa-v-united-states-1922 https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/260/178

[E-W5-063-01] W5 1923 legal TA
United States v. Thind - Caucasian is not what white means
February 19, 1923. Three months after Ozawa, the Supreme Court held Bhagat Singh Thind ineligible for naturalisation although he was argued to be Caucasian. The Court reasoned that 'the words white persons are words of common speech', to be read by common understanding rather than scientific definition, and that 'Caucasian is a conventional word of much flexibility' whose popular sense is narrower than its scientific one. Ozawa was excluded for not being Caucasian; Thind was excluded because Caucasian is not what the statute means.
sources: [A live_verified] src-us-v-thind-1923 https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/261/204

[E-W5-064-01] W5 1861 military TA
American Civil War begins
April 1861. Armed conflict began between the United States and the seceding states. This entry exists because two W4 dossiers, on the strengthened Fugitive Slave Act and the Kansas-Nebraska Act, both reference the war by event_id as the downstream terminus of the sectional-partition chain and could not resolve it. W5 separately carries the Sine Die adjournment of March 1861 and wartime censorship, but the war itself had no entry.
sources: [A live_verified] src-lincoln-proclamation-80-1861 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/12/1258

[E-W5-065-01] W5 1873 financial TA MILESTONE
Coinage Act: the standard silver dollar omitted from the statutory list
12 February 1873. Section 15 enumerates the silver coins of the United States as a trade-dollar, a half-dollar, a quarter-dollar and a dime, and makes those coins legal tender only for amounts not exceeding five dollars in any one payment. The standard silver dollar does not appear in the enumeration. Section 17 then provides that no coins of gold, silver or minor coinage shall thereafter be issued from the mint other than those of the denominations, standards and weights set forth in the Act. The two sections together end the free coinage of the standard silver dollar by omission and closure rather than by express repeal, which is why the effect was contested afterward and why the statute acquired the name it carries. The mechanism is definitional: the operative act is what the enumeration leaves out.
sources: [A live_verified] src-us-stat-17-424-coinage-act-1873 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/17/424

[E-W5-066-01] W5 1858 corporate TA MILESTONE
The Company's trusteeship terminated, not its sovereignty transferred
2 August 1858. The Act's preamble records that under the legislation of 1853 the territories in the possession and under the government of the East India Company had been continued under that government in trust for Her Majesty, and that the property and rights concerned were held by the Company in trust for Her Majesty for the purposes of that government. Section 1 provides that the government of those territories, and all powers in relation to government vested in or exercised by the Company in trust for Her Majesty, shall cease to be vested in or exercised by the Company. The operative act is therefore the termination of a trusteeship over territory the Crown already held beneficially, and not the transfer of sovereignty from a corporation to a state. The trust language is in the statute.
sources: [A live_verified] src-uk-21-22-vict-c106-government-of-india-1858 https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/Vict/21-22/106/pdfs/ukpga_18580106_en.pdf

[E-W5-066-02] W5 1858 legal TA MILESTONE
Direct rule substituted for chartered administration
The powers that had been exercised by a chartered joint stock company are vested in the Crown and exercised in its name. The corporate form that Windows 1 and 2 build from the Muscovy and Levant companies through the East India Company of 1600 reaches its terminal node here, in the dissolution of the founding instance's governing function.
sources: [A live_verified] src-uk-21-22-vict-c106-government-of-india-1858 https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/Vict/21-22/106/pdfs/ukpga_18580106_en.pdf

[E-W5-067-01] W5 1871 legal TA MILESTONE
Indian Appropriations proviso: the treaty category closed, existing treaties expressly preserved
3 March 1871. The proviso attached to the Indian Appropriations Act states that hereafter no Indian nation or tribe within the territory of the United States shall be acknowledged or recognized as an independent nation, tribe or power with whom the United States may contract by treaty. The category is closed going forward. The same proviso then continues that nothing contained in it shall be construed to invalidate or impair the obligation of any treaty heretofore lawfully made and ratified with any such Indian nation or tribe. Both halves are in one sentence. The instrument is therefore prospective closure with express preservation, not abrogation, and the distinction is the whole of its legal effect. Reading only the first half overstates what the statute did; reading both gives the ruling exactly. The firewall established in 1787 survives 1871 because the drafters of 1871 said so in the act that closed the door behind it.
sources: [A live_verified] src-us-stat-16-566-indian-approps-1871 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/16/566

[E-W5-069-01] W5 1866 military TA
Army Reorganization Act: six regiments of coloured men established by statute
28 July 1866. The act increasing and fixing the military peace establishment provides for two regiments of cavalry and four regiments of infantry to be composed of coloured men. Two plus four is six, which confirms the count carried in the source register against the statutory text rather than against secondary description. The provision sits inside a general reorganisation of the peacetime army, which means the same instrument that sets the establishment for the post-war period also creates a permanent segregated formation within it. Those regiments, after the 1869 reorganisation consolidated the four infantry regiments into two, are the force subsequently deployed in the western campaigns. The statute itself states no such purpose, and the corpus records the sequence without asserting the intent: the establishment is created here, the deployment follows, and the connection between them is not supplied by this document.
sources: [A live_verified] src-us-stat-14-332-army-reorg-1866 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/14/332; [B live_verified] src-w5cc-national-archives-prologue-spring-2001-trevor-1a90 https://www.archives.gov/publications/prologue/2001/spring/buffalo-soldiers.html

[E-W5-070-01] W5 1858 corporate TA MILESTONE
The East India Company petitions the Lords, and the phrase used in presenting it names an instrument of governing an empire
11 February 1858. The East India Company petitions the House of Lords against the transfer of the government of India to the Crown. Presenting it, Earl Grey describes the Government's measure as "a Bill to abolish the East India Company as an instrument of governing that empire", and summarises the petition rather than reading it. The phrase is the presenting peer's, not the Company's, and the petition itself is never read into the record. Six months later the Government of India Act terminates the arrangement. What that act ends is a trusteeship over territory the Crown already held beneficially, not a sovereignty passing from a company to a state, and the phrase used in presenting the Company's petition is the clearest contemporaneous description of what the withdrawn instrument had been doing.
sources: [A live_verified] src-hansard-hl-1858-02-11-eic-petition https://hansard.parliament.uk/Lords/1858-02-11; [A live_verified] src-w5cc-hl-deb-11-february-1858-vol-148-cc1121-64-the-4759 https://api.parliament.uk/historic-hansard/lords/1858/feb/11/the-east-india-companys-petition; [A live_verified] src-w5cc-hc-deb-09-february-1858-vol-148-cc970-5-the-ea-a57f https://api.parliament.uk/historic-hansard/commons/1858/feb/09/the-east-india-companys-petition

[E-W5-071-01] W5 1862 legal TA MILESTONE
Coolie Trade Act - sec. 4 invents the consular certificate
19 February 1862, 12 Stat. 340. Prohibits American vessels carrying subjects of China to be held to service. Then sec. 4 builds the exception: a consular certificate that the emigration is voluntary. THE HINGE OF THE INDENTURE CHAIN. Thirteen years later the Page Act reaches back for that same certificate and changes only the test the consul applies. Prohibition and pre-clearance cut from one part.
sources: [A live_verified] src-us-12-340-coolie-trade-1862 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/12/340; [B live_verified] src-page-act-1875 https://loveman.sdsu.edu/docs/1875Immigration%20Act.pdf; [A live_verified] src-frus-1865p2-d551-burlingame-consular https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1865p2/d551

[E-W5-072-01] W5 1868 legal TA MILESTONE
Burlingame Treaty - residence granted, naturalization withheld, same article
28 July 1868, 16 Stat. 739. Reciprocal rights of residence and travel granted. Naturalization withheld by express words IN THE SAME ARTICLE. Signed nineteen days after the Fourteenth Amendment is ratified. Two years later the Naturalization Act extends the privilege to aliens of African nativity and descent and says nothing of Asians. What 1868 reserved in words, 1870 accomplished by silence.
sources: [A live_verified] src-us-16-739-burlingame-1868 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/16/739; [A live_verified] src-us-stat-16-254-naturalization-1870 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/16/254; [A live_verified] src-18th-amendment-1919 https://www.archives.gov/founding-docs/amendments-11-27

[E-W5-073-01] W5 1863 legal TA
Dakota treaties abrogated - the instrument, five months after the fighting ended
16 February 1863, 12 Stat. 652. Treaties with the Sisseton, Wahpeton, Medawakanton and Wahpakoota bands declared abrogated and annulled. Lands and annuities forfeited. The fighting of 1862 is the trigger. THIS STATUTE IS THE INSTRUMENT. Separate events. The land transfer and the extinguishment of standing happen here, in text, some five months after the fighting ended.
sources: [A live_verified] src-us-12-652-dakota-abrogation-1863 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/12/652; [B live_verified] src-w5cc-mnopedia-minnesota-historical-society-us-dakot-abc1 https://www.mnhs.org/mnopedia/search/index/event/us-dakota-war-1862

[E-W5-074-01] W5 1862 land TA MILESTONE
Morrill Act - public land capitalised into permanent endowment
2 July 1862, 12 Stat. 503. Thirty thousand acres per senator and representative, granted to the states. Proceeds invested, income applied to colleges. Not sale for revenue. CAPITALISATION. The land stops being territory and becomes a financial instrument attached to a corporate body that does not die.
sources: [A live_verified] src-us-12-503-morrill-1862 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/12/503

[E-W5-075-01] W5 1862 land TA
Pacific Railway Act - land, credit and military logistics fused in one statute
1 July 1862, 12 Stat. 489. Charters a company. Grants alternate sections of public land along the route. Issues bonds against the work. Requires line and telegraph to carry government traffic, troops and munitions included. FOUR FUNCTIONS IN ONE INSTRUMENT: land transfer, corporate creation, public credit, military logistics. The Indian telegraph franchise debated in the Commons five years earlier runs the same structure abroad, a state guaranteeing the return on a private spine it does not build.
sources: [A live_verified] src-us-12-489-pacific-railway-1862 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/12/489; [A live_verified] src-hansard-hc-1857-03-21-telegraph-india https://api.parliament.uk/historic-hansard/commons/1857/mar/21/question-2

[E-W5-076-01] W5 1865 legal TA
Freedmen's Bureau Act - the sunset is in the founding section
3 March 1865, 13 Stat. 507. The bureau is established in the War Department, to continue during the present war of rebellion and for one year thereafter. THE LIMIT IS IN THE FOUNDING SECTION. Not imposed later. This corrects an implication the corpus carries elsewhere, that the bureau was killed in 1872 by a hostile act. The terminal date was written at the creation.
sources: [A live_verified] src-us-13-507-freedmens-bureau-1865 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/13/507

[E-W5-077-01] W5 1867 legal TA MILESTONE
Alaska cession Article III - a classification clause inside the transferring instrument
30 March 1867, 15 Stat. 539. Article III admits the inhabitants to the rights of citizens, EXCEPTING uncivilized native tribes, who are to be subject to such laws as the United States may from time to time adopt. The instrument that transfers the territory divides the people on it into two standings. One enumerated. One left to future administration. Not a later administrative gloss. It is in the cession itself.
sources: [A live_verified] src-us-15-539-alaska-1867 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/15/539

[E-W5-077-02] W5 1867 population TA MILESTONE
Alaska cession Article III - a classification clause inside the transferring instrument

Article III admits the inhabitants to the rights of citizens, EXCEPTING uncivilized native tribes, who are to be subject to such laws as the United States may from time to time adopt. [E-W5-078-01] W5 1867 sovereign TA MILESTONE
British North America Act - franchise transferred, sovereignty retained
29 March 1867, 30 and 31 Vict. c. 3. Federates the provinces into one Dominion under the Crown. NOT AN INDEPENDENCE INSTRUMENT. It is a franchise transfer: internal self-government devolved, the amending power and external sovereignty retained at Westminster. The Dominion is created by an act of the imperial Parliament, which is itself the proof of where the constituent power sits.
sources: [A live_verified] src-uk-30-31-vict-c3-bna-1867 https://www.legislation.gov.uk/ukpga/Vict/30-31/3/pdfs/ukpga_18670003_en.pdf

[E-W5-079-01] W5 1871 financial TB
German Coinage Act sec. 10 - demonetisation by express prohibition
RGBl. 1871 p. 404. Section 10 prohibits the coinage of coarse silver. EXPRESS PROHIBITION, stated in words. Fourteen months later the United States reaches the same result by omission and closure and repeals nothing. Two jurisdictions, one movement, two drafting techniques. The American Commission of 1878 narrates them as a single chain.
sources: [B live_verified] src-rgbl-1871-404-goldmuenzen https://de.wikisource.org/wiki/Gesetz,_betreffend_die_Ausprägung_von_Reichsgoldmünzen; [A live_verified] src-us-stat-17-424-coinage-act-1873 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/17/424; [B live_verified] src-imc-1878-american-commission https://archive.org/download/internationalmo00statgoog/internationalmo00statgoog_djvu.txt

[E-W5-080-01] W5 1856 land TA
Ley Lerdo Art. 3 - every perpetual corporation disentailed
25 June 1856. Article 3 reaches beyond the Church. It names ayuntamientos and EVERY FOUNDATION OF PERPETUAL OR INDEFINITE DURATION, so municipal common lands and village corporate holdings fall inside the same definition as ecclesiastical estates. Article 8 then carves out the buildings each corporation uses for its own institutional purpose, and, for municipal property, the ejidos and land kept exclusively for a town's public service. The statute is read as anticlerical; its breadth is corporate. What is left, on both sides of the line, is land held by a body that does not die, converted into land held by persons who do.
sources: [A live_verified] src-unam-ley-lerdo-1856 https://archivos.juridicas.unam.mx/www/bjv/libros/12/5625/17.pdf; [C live_verified] src-w5cc-encyclopedia-of-latin-american-history-and-cul-084a https://www.encyclopedia.com/humanities/encyclopedias-almanacs-transcripts-and-maps/ley-lerdo

[E-W5-081-01] W5 1863 land TA
New Zealand Settlements Act - the taking in sec. 4, compensation denied by sec. 6
3 December 1863. Districts may be declared and land taken where a tribe is judged to have been in rebellion. Section 4 is the taking: land passes to the Crown the moment the Governor in Council declares it required for the purposes of the Act. Section 6 is the punitive hinge on the other side of it: those who will not come in and submit to trial are denied compensation for what has been taken. THE INSTRUMENT IS THE OPERATIVE ACT, NOT THE FIGHTING. The war supplies the predicate. The statute performs the transfer, and it does so by the legislature of the settler colony.
sources: [A live_verified] src-nz-settlements-act-1863 https://waikatotainui.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/12/New-Zealand-Settlement-Act.pdf

[E-W5-082-01] W5 1860 legal TB
Convention of Peking Article V - the traffic acquires treaty backing
24 October 1860. Article V provides for Chinese subjects to contract for service abroad and for carriage in British vessels. IMPERIAL DECREE BACKING IN TREATY TEXT. What the 1862 US act would call a traffic to be prohibited, the 1860 convention makes lawful carriage two years earlier. By 1871 the Commons is calling the same trade four names in one afternoon.
sources: [B live_verified] src-peking-1860-art-v https://worldjpn.net/documents/texts/pw/18601024.T1E.html; [A live_verified] src-us-12-340-coolie-trade-1862 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/12/340; [A live_verified] src-hansard-hc-1871-06-19-macao-coolie https://api.parliament.uk/historic-hansard/commons/1871/jun/19/question-1

[E-W5-083-01] W5 1875 legal TB
Page Act - the 1862 certificate reused, only the test changed
3 March 1875. A consular pre-clearance regime for emigration from China, Japan and any Oriental country. THE INSTRUMENT IS THE 1862 CERTIFICATE. What changes is the test the consul applies, from whether the emigration is voluntary to whether the emigrant is of a class to be barred. Thirteen years separate the two statutes. The machinery is continuous.

[E-W5-084-01] W5 1859 military TA
The Reform War, and British claims lodged against its outcome
HC Deb, 8 July 1859. The Mexican civil war described in the Commons, with British-subject grievances registered while it is still running. The claims are on the record BEFORE the intervention that will later be justified by them. Sequence matters here. The grievance is contemporaneous; the use made of it is not.

[E-W5-085-01] W5 1861 financial TA MILESTONE
London Convention - three powers against one sovereign debtor
31 October 1861. Britain, France and Spain concert a combined operation against Mexico over suspended debt service. THE TREATY IS CONCLUDED IN THE CAPITAL OF ONE OF THE THREE. Russell signs for Britain at London, with Flahault for France and Isturiz for Spain. Debt is the stated predicate and force is the instrument, and the forum belongs to a creditor.

[E-W5-086-01] W5 1862 financial TA
Forced loans, named and called a euphemism in the same breath
HC Deb, 10 March 1862. The claims behind the intervention debt are described as forced loans, and the word loan is marked in the House as a euphemism as it is spoken. FOUR WEEKS BEFORE the allied powers separate at Orizaba. The mechanism generating the debt is named on the record by the legislature of one of the intervening powers.

[E-W5-087-01] W5 1862 financial TA
The Jecker bonds - a debt instrument converted into an occupation
The Jecker claim, admitted on the record of the intervening power's own legislature. Bonds procurable at a third of their nominal value were receivable at par for a fifth of every Mexican customs duty, and the resulting claim became one of the causes on which the Soledad convention broke. THE CLAIM IS ADMITTED IN THE CHAMBER; THE DISCOUNT IS ON THE FRENCH ARMY'S OWN RECORD. Debt instrument to occupation.

[E-W5-088-01] W5 1867 population TA MILESTONE
Nationality stripped by administrative operation, enforced name by name
Reported to the Department, 1867. A French marshal's circular recalls the French in Mexican service and puts them to an election, and the forfeiture behind it is article 21 of the Civil Code: a Frenchman who takes foreign military service without the government's leave loses his French status. NOT A COURT. A STATUTE OF 1804, SET RUNNING BY A MILITARY CIRCULAR AND ENFORCED OFF A NAME-BY-NAME ROLL. The population affected is European, which is what makes this the clearest instance in the band of the mechanism working independently of the classification it is usually attached to.

[E-W5-089-01] W5 1867 records TA
The count of those who stayed, twelve days after the despatch
Twelve days after the despatch that carried the circular, the same consul reports the number of foreign soldiers who stayed in imperial service after refusing the offer of a return to Europe. THE MECHANISM IS COUNTED BY A NEUTRAL CONSUL. One document carries the instrument, the next counts who stayed, and both sit in the same published volume under the same signature.

[E-W5-090-01] W5 1867 sovereign TA
Queretaro - a terminal negotiation conducted for other people's standing
1867. As the last French troops leave, a displaced sovereign rides for Queretaro, and the United States consul reports the impression that he goes to put propositions to Juarez, his main desire being GUARANTEES FOR THOSE COMPROMISED BY SERVICE TO HIM, and his own position resigned. Not terms for himself. No such negotiation was ever conducted: the city was besieged from 5 March, fell on 15 May, and he was shot on 19 June 1867. What was at stake was classification after the fall, not survival of the throne.

[E-W5-091-01] W5 1871 sovereign TA MILESTONE
The constitutive act was 1 January 1871, not 18 January
Bancroft to the Department, 1871. THE CONSTITUTIVE ACT WAS 1 JANUARY. Versailles on 18 January was the ceremony. The empire comes into being by an instrument taking effect at the turn of the year, and the proclamation in the hall of mirrors is the announcement of something already done. A constitutive act made procedurally, then dressed.
sources: [A live_verified] src-frus-1871-d172-bancroft https://history.state.gov/historicaldocuments/frus1871/d172; [B live_verified] src-w5cc-proclamation-of-the-german-empire-english-wiki-a966 https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proclamation_of_the_German_Empire

[E-W5-092-01] W5 1871 land TA
Secret bargaining over a third party's land, printed in a US public document
1871. A draft treaty in which two powers bargain over territory belonging to neither, printed in full in Foreign Relations of the United States for 1870, where Bancroft's despatch of 27 July 1870 encloses a translation of all five articles. The secrecy failed in London, in the Times of 25 July 1870, when Bismarck released the draft after war had come. Third parties' land is the consideration, and the United States volume preserves the text two days later in a free government publication.

[E-W5-093-01] W5 1857 military TA
The Commons defeats its own government on the war it is prosecuting
HC Deb, 26 February and 3 March 1857. The opening of the Second Opium War is censured by motion, and on the division THE GOVERNMENT IS DEFEATED BY ITS OWN LEGISLATURE IN THE WAR'S OPENING MONTHS. Not a post hoc inquiry. A vote against the conduct of a war while that war is being fought, recorded in the chamber that funds it.

[E-W5-094-01] W5 1861 records TA
The Summer Palace burning, admitted and defended with a status distinction
HC Deb, 14 February 1861. The burning of the Emperor of China's Summer Palace, with the imperial collections and one of the seven imperial copies of the Siku Quanshu inside it, is admitted, named and defended on a vote of thanks. A STATUS DISTINCTION IS OFFERED IN JUSTIFICATION. The destruction is entered on the record of the destroying power, with a reason given about whose property it was.

The naming of the Taiping contested in a third power's legislature

[E-W5-099-01] W5 1871 legal TA
A British court calls the traffic slavery, and the executive re-arrests the man it freed
HC Deb, 19 June 1871. The coolie traffic is ADJUDICATED AS SLAVERY BY A BRITISH COURT. The man the court releases is then re-arrested by the executive. Judicial finding and administrative action point opposite ways on the same facts, in the same jurisdiction, in the same year, and both are in the record.

Four names for one traffic in a single afternoon
HC Deb, 19 June 1871, two questions in one sitting (cc220-1 and c222). The same trade is called the Coolie Immigration Service, irregular practices, a code of regulations, and slavery. FOUR NAMES, ONE AFTERNOON. The naming variance is not confusion. It is the range of positions available to speakers who all know what is being described.

The empire's communications spine let as a private franchise

[E-W5-102-01] W5 1857 military TA
Two theatres supplied from one pool, diverted at a waypoint
HC Deb, 10 and 13 July 1857. The Governor General of India asks, on his own responsibility, that troops on their way to China be diverted at Ceylon; the minister tells the House on 10 July that no such orders were sent, and three days later the Prime Minister confirms the request and the Government's approval of it. Two theatres drawing on a single pool of men, and the allocation decided in transit by someone outside the chain that dispatched them. The China war and the Indian rising are not separate operations at the level of supply.

The next day the complaint is that an inference was allowed to stand uncorrected, made by the man who led the opposing party in the Commons. The information gap is on the record from both benches.

Standing granted, revoked, and preserved for those who relied on it
HC Deb, 16 July 1857. An amnesty is granted by administrative order, then revoked, and standing is PRESERVED FOR A DEFINED WINDOW for those who had already acted in reliance on it. Three operations on the same instrument: creation, withdrawal, and a saving for reliance. All by order, none by statute.

[E-W5-106-01] W5 1862 sovereign TA
Recognition withheld prospectively by standing order
Dispatch of 1862. Recognition is treated as an instrument and withheld BY STANDING ORDER, prospectively, against a named contingency that does not arrive for nearly two years. Not a decision taken when the question arises. A rule set in advance about a case that has not yet occurred, which is what makes recognition legible here as a lever rather than an acknowledgement.

[E-W5-110-01] W5 1867 population TA
The emigration proposal live at Cabinet level, sixteen months after the Thirteenth Amendment
Dispatch of 1867. A proposal to fund the emigration of freedmen to Liberia is kept alive by the Secretary of State, who refers it to Congress and asks to be reminded of it, SIXTEEN MONTHS AFTER the Thirteenth Amendment was ratified. Emancipation and removal are not sequential policies where one replaces the other. They run concurrently, and the later one is still being discussed at the top of government when the earlier is settled law.

Sovereignty argued as divisible rather than extinguished, spiritual retained and temporal transferred
HL Deb, 19 April 1861. A sovereignty is argued over as something that could be DIVIDED RATHER THAN EXTINGUISHED, spiritual authority retained and temporal authority transferred over the same ground. The division is put as the thing to be arranged, and the Government declines to arrange it. A holder can be stripped of one half of what he holds while remaining the holder of the other, and the record treats that as coherent.

[E-W5-127-01] W5 1866 innovation TA
The Atlantic cable becomes operative, fixed to a date and a physical object
HC Deb, 27 July 1866. The link becomes operative, fixed to a date, a chamber and a physical object. An 1858 debate records a different company's North Sea cable, its physical footprint off the Kent coast and who bore its nuisance. With the Indian telegraph franchise of 1857, where the guarantee is stated from the Treasury bench, the communications spine of the period is documented as PRIVATE INFRASTRUCTURE CARRYING PUBLIC GUARANTEES.

[E-W6-001-01] W6 1921 financial TB

November 23, 1921. First federal social welfare program. Provides FEDERAL FUNDING to states for maternal and infant health. THE MECHANISM: to receive federal funds, states must establish UNIFORM BIRTH REGISTRATION. By 1929, every state has a standardized birth certificate system. THE FORENSIC READING: The Act is Layer 2 of the Matryoshka. The 14th Amendment (1868) created the jus soli citizen-class. The 1921 Act creates the DOCUMENTATION SYSTEM that registers every person born into that class. The birth certificate is not a health document - it is a financial instrument. It creates a “legal person” (the UPPERCASE name on the certificate) distinct from the living being. That legal person is a MUNICIPAL BOND - a revenue-generating unit within the Corporate ledger. Each registered birth creates a future taxpayer, debtor, and collateral unit. The 14th Amendment supplies the constitutional authority. The 1921 Act supplies the registration mechanism. The 1933 bankruptcy (HJR 192) supplies the moment when the registered citizen-class becomes the SOLE COLLATERAL for national debt. CHAIN: 14th Amendment (1868, citizen-class created) → 1870 Naturalization Act (jus soli overwrite) → 1871 DC Organic Act (Corporation installed) → 1913 Federal Reserve (Collection Engine) → HERE (registration mechanism) → 1924 Plecker (racial classification on birth certificates) → 1933 HJR 192 (citizen-class becomes debt collateral) → 1935 SSN (permanent individual identifier). [E-W6-002-01] W6 1933 financial TA
BANKRUPTCY #3 - HJR 192, Gold Seizure & McFadden Charges
THREE INSTRUMENTS, SAME YEAR: (1) Executive Order 6102 (April 5) requires all persons to deliver their gold coin, gold bullion and gold certificates to the Federal Reserve by May 1, 1933, exempting up to $100 each (EO 6102). (2) House Joint Resolution 192 (June 5) voids gold clauses: an obligation containing one is discharged dollar for dollar in any legal tender (31 USC 5118(d)). The corpus reads this as making the people’s labor the collateral for perpetual debt. (3) The Emergency Banking Act (March 9) rewrites section 5(b) of the Act of October 6, 1917, the Trading with the Enemy Act, so that in a declared national emergency the President may regulate gold and foreign-exchange transactions 'by any person within the United States' (48 Stat. 1). The corpus reads this as a wartime power turned on the government’s own people; the text does not call them enemies. THE CORPUS’S READING, THREE BANKRUPTCIES: #1 (1789) - Constitution as debt reorganization, First Bank as receiver. #2 (1861) - Sine Die, de jure Republic dissolves, 14th Amendment creates new collateral base. #3 (1933) - HJR 192, gold seized, citizen-labor becomes sole collateral. The 1787 Treaty survives ALL THREE. International law instruments are not dissolved by the bankruptcy of one party. The Corporation has been bankrupt three times. The Heirs’ standing is not.

[E-W6-016-01] W6 1968 legal TA
Dyett v. Turner - a Utah justice attacks the 14th Amendment's ratification, and disclaims asking to void it
22 March 1968. In Dyett v. Turner (439 P.2d 266), the Utah Supreme Court denies a bad-check prisoner's habeas petition on the ground that he waived counsel. The lead opinion, by Justice Ellett, goes further and attacks the amendment's ratification as the footing for the United States Supreme Court's "usurpation of the prerogatives of the states" on school prayer, apportionment and appointed counsel, while saying in terms: "We have no desire at this time to have the Fourteenth Amendment declared unconstitutional. In fact, we are not asked to do that." Its argument: seven southern states ratified "by spurious, nonrepresentative governments... under the duress of military occupation and of being denied representation in Congress"; states that had rejected it were counted after ratifying under that duress; Ohio and New Jersey withdrew their ratifications and were counted anyway; and Seward's proclamation of 20 July 1868 certified ratification only conditionally, until Congress declared the amendment adopted by concurrent resolution the next day. ONE OTHER JUSTICE JOINED THAT REASONING; THREE CONCURRED IN THE RESULT ONLY. The ratification argument was the judge's, not the prisoner's, and was not the ground of decision; the federal court that heard the case next did not address it. The corpus had recorded this as a 1967 Utah legislative memorial to Congress; no such memorial is in the 1967 Congressional Record, and none has yet been found in Utah's own records. The corpus's reading, not the court's: if the amendment was never lawfully ratified, the jus soli citizen-class, the DC Organic Act of 1871 and the collateral base the corpus reads into 1933 lose their constitutional footing, and every layer of the Matryoshka with them.
sources: [A live_verified] src-dyett-v-turner-1968-utah https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/1272144/dyett-v-turner/; [A live_verified] src-dyett-v-turner-1968-d-utah https://www.courtlistener.com/opinion/8782422/dyett-v-turner/

audit trail: UTAH MEMORIAL SEARCHED IN THE 1967 CONGRESSIONAL RECORD AND NOT FOUND 2026-08-01 (Thread 20 W6 batch 7). NOTHING ATTACHED. What was done: the full-year CONGRESSIONAL RECORD INDEX for 1967, volume 113, was downloaded and read, 17,031,268 characters, together with the two 1967 volumes covering late February to mid March. THREE SPECIFIC NEGATIVES, each from the index's own headings. (1) Under 'Resolutions of State legislatures proposing amendments' the index lists NINE STATES and UTAH IS NOT AMONG THEM: Alabama, Colorado, Georgia, Illinois, Indiana, Massachusetts, Nevada, North Dakota, Texas. Their subjects are tax sharing, apportionment, a constitutional convention, presidential electors and prayer in schools. (2) Under the heading 'Fourteenth Amendment' the index carries exactly ONE relevant item, and it is not Utah's: 'Fourteenth Amendment Is Unconstitutional, Leander H. Perez, 15641', an inserted report by a Louisiana figure. (3) The index DOES list, under Senator Wallace F. Bennett of Utah, 'Memorials of legislature: Utah, 5521'. THAT PAGE WAS FETCHED AND READ. It is a Utah resolution about water: it directs the Utah Secretary of State to transmit copies 'to the President of the United States, to the Secretary of the Interior, to the Director of the Bureau of the Budget, TO THE UPPER COLORADO RIVER COMMISSION and to the Senators and Congressmen representing the State of Utah'. It has nothing to do with the Fourteenth Amendment. SO THE ONE INDEXED UTAH LEGISLATIVE MEMORIAL IN THE 1967 CONGRESSIONAL RECORD IS ABOUT THE COLORADO RIVER. WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT PROVE, stated carefully. It does NOT prove the Utah legislature passed no such memorial. A state legislature can adopt a memorial that Congress never prints or indexes, and the passage would still appear in Utah's own session laws and legislative journals. What it does show is that the memorial is not in the 1967 Congressional Record where the entry's framing places it, and that E-W6-016-02's claim of press invisibility cannot be tested until the underlying document is located. THE ROUTE THAT WOULD SETTLE IT, and it is not the Congressional Record: the Laws of Utah 1967 and the Journals of the Utah House and Senate for the 37th Legislature, held by the Utah State Archives. The author should also confirm the YEAR; the claim circulates attached to more than one date. | RESOLVED 2026-09-21 (Thread 33, Utah entry), on the author's approval of UTAH_ENTRY_DRAFT.md. The document the entry describes is Dyett v. Turner, 439 P.2d 266 (Utah Supreme Court, 22 March 1968): a court opinion, not a legislative memorial, and 1968, not 1967. Its lead opinion carries all four of the entry's arguments; one other justice joined the reasoning and three concurred in the result only; and it says in terms that it does not ask for the amendment to be declared unconstitutional. The Thread 20 search record above stands. This entry's own claim, national press silence, is untested against either object and is marked "(not yet sourced)". ALSO STILL OPEN: whether a separate Utah legislative memorial exists; route the Laws of Utah 1967 and the 37th Legislature's journals, Utah State Archives. Also unread: the Leander H. Perez insertion at 1967 Congressional Record 15641, the likely source of the "1967" and "Congressional Record" in the original entry.

[E-W6-030-01] W6 2026b financial TB
Trump v. Barbara - Birthright citizenship before the Supreme Court
April 1, 2026. Oral arguments. President Trump attends in person - first sitting president to attend Supreme Court oral arguments. The administration challenged the 14th Amendment’s birthright citizenship clause, arguing it should not apply to children of undocumented immigrants. DECIDED 30 JUNE 2026, AND THE ADMINISTRATION LOST. The Court AFFIRMED the judgment below and struck down Executive Order 14160, holding, from the syllabus: 'Children born in the United States to parents unlawfully or temporarily present are subject to the jurisdiction of the United States and are citizens at birth under the Fourteenth Amendment's Citizenship Clause.' Roberts C.J. delivered the opinion of the Court, joined by Sotomayor, Kagan, Barrett and Jackson; Kavanaugh concurred in the judgment and dissented in part; Thomas, Alito and Gorsuch dissented. FIVE JUSTICES MADE THE OPINION AND A SIXTH CONCURRED IN THE JUDGMENT ONLY, so no vote fraction describes this case: two independent secondary reports gave it as 6 to 3 and as 5 to 4 and neither is correct. State the lineup, not a count. THE REAL TRIGGER: the municipal bond market is collapsing. Cities are going bankrupt. Nations have stopped buying U.S. Treasury bonds. The birthright citizenship debate is before the Supreme Court because the BOND SYSTEM is failing - not because of any moral concern about immigration. Each birth certificate represents a revenue unit in the municipal bond market. If the citizen-class shrinks, the collateral base for municipal and sovereign debt shrinks with it. Trump wants to NARROW the pool. The ACLU wants to MAINTAIN it. NEITHER SIDE IS ASKING THE THRESHOLD QUESTIONS: (1) Was the 14th Amendment ever lawfully ratified? (Argued against by two of five Utah Supreme Court justices in Dyett v. Turner, 1968, who expressly declined to ask that it be voided; not the ground of that decision, and not addressed by the federal court that heard the case next.) (2) Were the populations it covers lawfully subject to its jurisdiction? (3) Where does the revenue generated by the citizen-class actually go? (4) Is the citizen-class collateral or beneficiary? Both sides argue within the 14th Amendment framework as if it is legitimate. Neither questions whether the Matryoshka’s foundation is sound. The forensic reading: this case is about the SIZE OF THE COLLATERAL POOL, not the rights of the people in it.
