[E-W6-030-03] W6 2026b legal TB
14th Amendment under direct challenge
For the first time since Wong Kim Ark (1898), the scope of the 14th Amendment’s birthright clause went directly before the Court, and on 30 June 2026 the Court decided it. Wong Kim Ark is cited 115 times in the slip opinion, which settles it as the governing precedent the parties fought over, and the majority holds it 'was merely declaratory of the fundamental rule of citizenship by birth that prevailed at common law', reasoning from Blackstone, Calvin's Case and the condemnation of Dred Scott. A Utah Supreme Court opinion (Dyett v. Turner, 1968) challenged its ratification. McFadden (1933) challenged the financial architecture built on it. Now the executive branch challenges its scope. Three different angles of attack across 93 years - none asking the fundamental question: was the instrument ever lawfully installed?

[E-W6-031-01] W6 2020 legal TA
McGirt v. Oklahoma - the reservation was never disestablished
July 9, 2020. The Supreme Court held 5-4 that the Muscogee (Creek) Nation reservation in eastern Oklahoma was never disestablished by Congress, so roughly the eastern half of the state remains Indian country for purposes of the Major Crimes Act. Gorsuch, writing for the majority, opened on the premise that only Congress can disestablish a reservation and that it had not done so here. The decision reaches the Oklahoma Enabling Act of 1906 and the allotment statutes that W5 sources, and it cited Worcester v. Georgia.
sources: [A live_verified] src-mcgirt-v-oklahoma-2020 https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/18-9526

[E-W6-031-02] W6 2020 land TA
Eastern Oklahoma remains Indian country
The holding did not transfer land. It established that a jurisdictional boundary the state had treated as extinguished for over a century remained in force, which is a finding about the PERSISTENCE of a treaty line rather than about ownership. Subsequent litigation narrowed the criminal jurisdiction consequences without disturbing the boundary finding.

[E-W6-032-01] W6 1965 legal TA
Immigration and Nationality Act of 1965 - national origins repealed
October 3, 1965. The Hart-Celler Act abolished the national-origins quota system installed by the Immigration Act of 1924, which W5 sources at 43 Stat. 153. It replaced country-of-birth quotas with hemispheric caps and a preference system built on family reunification and occupational skill. The 1924 arithmetic of two percent of the 1890 census, and the 1921 three percent of 1910 before it, ceased to govern admission.
sources: [A live_verified] src-usc-8-1152-immigration-1965 https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/8/1152

[E-W6-037-01] W6 1974 financial TB
The ratings oligopoly - issuer-pays takes hold
During the early 1970s the major credit rating agencies shifted from a subscriber-pays model to an issuer-pays model, under which the entity being rated compensates the rater. The SEC's designation of Nationally Recognized Statistical Rating Organizations in 1975 entrenched the position of Moody's, Standard and Poor's and Fitch. construct-insurance-actuarial-sublane names this event; the corpus tracks the pricing of risk as a continuous instrument from Lloyd's forward.
sources: [A live_verified] src-credit-rating-agency-reform-act-2006 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/120/1327

[E-W6-038-01] W6 2001 financial TA
Credit default swaps: risk transfer without an ownership interest
Credit default swaps, developed through the 1990s and expanding sharply after 2000, transfer credit risk for a fee without requiring the buyer to hold the underlying obligation. The Commodity Futures Modernization Act of 2000 names the credit default swap in its definition of a swap agreement, and excludes such swaps, when negotiated privately between eligible contract participants, from the Commodity Exchange Act. Calling them insurance is the corpus's analogy, not the statute's. construct-insurance-actuarial-sublane names this event as a member of the actuarial chain.
sources: [A live_verified] src-cfma-2000-114-stat-2763 https://www.govinfo.gov/link/statute/114/2763

[E-W6-039-01] W6 2010s financial TB
Algorithmic risk scoring at population scale
The FICO score, whose origin W6 records at 1956, became through the 2010s one input among many in automated scoring systems reaching credit, insurance pricing, tenancy screening and employment checks. construct-insurance-actuarial-sublane names this as the chain's present terminus. The corpus's interest is that a risk score assigns a category to a person by formula, which is the actuarial operation it tracks from Lloyd's forward.
sources: [A live_verified] src-frb-credit-scoring-report-2007 https://www.federalreserve.gov/boarddocs/rptcongress/creditscore/creditscore.pdf

[E-W6-040-01] W6 2010s population TC
Consumer genetic testing at scale - ancestry as a product
Direct-to-consumer genetic testing expanded through the 2010s to tens of millions of participants. Ancestry results are reported against REFERENCE POPULATIONS assembled by each company, so the categories returned to a customer are the company's classification scheme rather than a fact about the genome. construct-whitening-project and construct-classification-franchise-protection both name this event; W6 separately records 23andMe at 2007, its retail launch year, and the A00 haplogroup at 2013; the company's own 10-K states that its accounting predecessor 'was incorporated in Delaware in 2006', so incorporation and launch are different years and the corpus means the launch.
sources: [A live_verified] src-23andme-10k-2024 https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1804591/000180459124000038/me-20240331.htm

[E-W6-040-02] W6 2010s information TB
The reference population is the classification
A test reports proximity to a reference panel. Where that panel is drawn determines what a result can say, and panels have been assembled unevenly by region and population. This entry exists because two constructs point at it and neither could resolve.

[E-W6-041-01] W6 2023 financial TB
Regional bank failures - Silicon Valley Bank and successors
March 2023. Silicon Valley Bank failed following a deposit run, the second largest bank failure in United States history at the time, followed within days by Signature Bank and in May by First Republic. Regulators invoked the systemic risk exception to guarantee uninsured deposits. dossier-tc-020-bubble points here from the South Sea Bubble, tracking the recurrence of a failure pattern rather than asserting a causal chain.

[w3-1652-counter-move] W3 1652 counter-move TC
Baptist and Quaker moral opposition documented as motivation for the 1652 Providence-Warwick statute (interpretive)
The Providence and Warwick towns had significant Baptist and Quaker populations whose religious teaching opposed slavery. Passage of the 1652 statute proves the political will existed at the town-assembly level. Non-enforcement proves the merchant-banker network (Tier 1 via Newport trade) prevailed over Tier 4 colonial elite dissent. This is the Chesapeake Class Split pattern (FW-011) executing on RI's colonial hierarchy thirty years before the 1682 Virginia race code that begins the Whitening Project timeline.

[w3-1652-legal] W3 1652 legal TA
Rhode Island Providence-Warwick anti-slavery statute passed by Assembly, structurally never enforced
The RI General Assembly at Providence and Warwick passes a statute limiting servitude to ten years, described in scholarship as the first-in-nation anti-slavery statute. Newport and Portsmouth are outside its reach, because they had separated under Coddington's commission and did not sit in that Assembly. The statute sets a forty-pound forfeiture, but there is no evidence it was ever enforced. The statute functions as legal cover, not legal prohibition. Track A of the three-track labor system operates undisturbed through Newport merchants for the entire fifty-one-year period between passage and the 1703 Assembly formal recognition of slavery.

[w3-1662-corporate] W3 5 July 1662 corporate TB
Asiento contracted to Genoese merchant bankers, with Dutch and English companies supplying the carriage
The Spanish crown contracts the asiento to Domenico Grillo and Ambrogio Lomellini, Genoese merchant bankers, for 24,000 piezas de India across seven years, about 3,430 a year. The Dutch West India Company and the English Royal Adventurers assist in supplying it. English participation in Asiento carriage therefore predates the 1713 transfer by fifty years, under a Genoese licence rather than a British one.

[w3-1701-sovereign] W3 1701 sovereign TB MILESTONE
Two crowns take quarter shareholdings in the asiento company itself, at 100,000 livres each
Philip V grants the asiento to the French Compagnie de Guinee et de l'Assiente under Jean-Baptiste du Casse, for 48,000 piezas de India across a decade. The company, founded in 1684, is reorganised in 1701 with the Spanish and French crowns each taking a quarter shareholding at 100,000 livres apiece. Both sovereigns hold capital in the operating company rather than licensing it from outside it.

[w3-1703-legal] W3 1703 legal TA MILESTONE
Rhode Island General Assembly formally recognizes slavery, structurally voids the 1652 Providence-Warwick statute
RI General Assembly passes (January 1703/4) a night-curfew act for 'negroes or Indians, either freemen, servants, or slaves', which historians read as the colony's statutory recognition of slavery; TIME describes it as superseding the 1652 statute, which the corpus reads as ending its fifty-one-year fiction. This is the franchise-transition moment for Track A of the three-track labor system in Rhode Island: the classification 'Negro' now has explicit colonial statutory backing rather than customary practice. Sets the stage for the 1708 monetization statute (TC-012) that puts an actual per-head duty on imports.

[w3-1704-financial] W3 1704 financial TA
Setup for 1713 Asiento franchise transfer
The Bourbon succession positions the crown to negotiate the 1713 Utrecht settlement in which the Asiento chattel-license transfers from Spanish-managed to British-managed operation. Read under FW-016 Merchant-Banker Constant: the Asiento reassignment is between sovereign holders at Tier 2/3 level, but the underlying Tier 1 merchant-banker beneficiary network is continuous across the transfer.

[w3-1704-sovereign] W3 1704 sovereign TA
Bourbon claimant holds the Spanish crown through a contested war
During the War of the Spanish Succession (1701-1714), the Bourbon claimant Philip V holds the Spanish crown through a contested war: his rival, the Archduke Charles, takes Barcelona in 1705 and enters Madrid in 1710, and the Utrecht settlement of 1713 confirms Philip as king. Bourbon control of both France and Spain (unified through family dynasty though separate crowns) sets the Tier-2 Senior-Lienholder configuration that persists through 1815. The Bourbon control of Spain is the immediate condition that makes the 1713 Utrecht Asiento reassignment to Britain legally possible: Spain-under-Bourbon-influence trades the Asiento to a competitor sovereign in exchange for territorial peace, transferring one classification franchise (chattel license) between sovereign holders.

[w3-1708-financial] W3 1708 financial TA
Rhode Island L3 duty becomes recurring revenue stream funding colonial administration
At the governor's 1708 estimate of 'betwixt twenty and thirty' imports a year, the three-pound duty yields some sixty to ninety pounds a year; by July 1715 the naval officer held 289 pounds 17 shillings in slave-import duties. Same event as w3-1708-legal but the financial-lane consequence: the classification is now generating tax revenue for the Tier 4 colonial government. The money stays in the colony: in 1715 it is granted to pave Newport's streets and build a bridge. The corpus reads the classification as a cash-generating asset for the colonial tier.

[w3-1708-legal] W3 1708 legal TA
Rhode Island General Assembly imposes L3 per-head duty on imported blacks
RI Assembly imposes a three-pound duty (New England current money) on every imported black person, formally monetizing Track A of the three-track labor system at the colonial statutory level. The duty is not a restriction; it is a tax revenue stream. The classification 'black' is now a taxable category in RI colonial law. This is the direct colonial-tier parallel to what Track C (Transportation Act 1717) will do to 'felon' classification ten years later at the imperial-tier level. Same code, different tier.

1711. The South Sea Company was incorporated and granted the Asiento contract, with a substantial portion of British government debt converted into company stock. [w3-1713-legal] W3 26 March 1713 legal TA MILESTONE
Asiento de Negros reassigned to Britain: 144,000 piezas de India across thirty years, Track A installed
The Asiento contract is signed by the Catholic King at Madrid on 26 March 1713, moving the monopoly on delivering enslaved Africans to Spanish America from French to British hands. Article I sets the term at thirty years, commencing 1 May 1713 and determining 1 May 1743, at 4,800 piezas de India a year and 144,000 in total. Article II fixes one composite duty of thirty three and one third per pieza covering all duties whatsoever. This is where Track A of the three-track labor system installs.

[w3-1714-legal] W3 1714 legal TA MILESTONE
Rhode Island passes runaway law and restricts Indian imports as classification-franchise protection (F1 prototype)
RI Assembly restricts Indian imports because Indian servants 'daily discouraged the importing of white servants from Great Britain.' This is the prototype instance of Classification-Franchise Protection (F1 Pattern, FW-009). The act's own stated reasons are crimes by Indian slaves and that their increase 'daily discourages the importing of white servants from Great Britain'; the corpus reads beyond them: the Managerial Class eliminates an ambiguous input (Indian servants with residual sovereign standing that could generate court challenges voiding the Negro classification) rather than defending the classification. Simultaneously RI installs a runaway law extending Track A enforcement into the internal geography. Both moves execute the same F1 logic. Direct forerunner of 1924 Plecker Racial Integrity Act's Moor-category elimination.

[w3-1717-financial] W3 March 1718 (Royal Assent) financial TA
Transportation Act financial architecture: Treasury per-head subsidy authorizes Track C contractor market
Same event as w3-1717-legal. Financial-lane consequence: the Transportation Act lets courts hand convicts to bonded contractors; the Treasury fixes a per-head payment administratively (the Treasury Lords 'agree to 3l. a head' on 5 August 1718), and the Robbery Act of 1719 then authorizes state payments, creating a Government-Guaranteed contractor market. This is the financial architecture that will underwrite the Jonathan Forward contract of 8 August 1718 (TC-017). The Treasury per-head payment is the direct analog of SSC per-head Asiento payment: same financial mechanism, different classification input.

[w3-1717-legal] W3 March 1718 (Royal Assent) legal TA MILESTONE
Transportation Act 4 Geo 1 c 11 receives Royal Assent, installing Track C domestic Asiento for English poor
The Transportation Act (Piracy Act) receives Royal Assent, installing the third rail of the three-track labor system. Track C converts English poor labeled 'felon' into 7 or 14 year property-transfer contracts. The statute uses the English benefit-of-clergy distinction (clergyable versus non-clergyable felonies) as the trigger mechanism for classification. This is the moment domestic Asiento becomes law: the English poor become tradeable property under identical logic to Track A (Asiento African trade) and Track B (Yamasee reclassification). Proves the classification code runs on race-neutral logic; only a legal category and a statute are required.

Coweta Resolution as counter-move to post-Yamasee reclassification, executed exactly when Track C is installing

[w3-1718-eagle-legal] W3 August 23 1718 legal TA MILESTONE
Eagle Galley departs Blackwall with 106 convicts bound for Virginia and Maryland, proof-of-concept for Track C
On August 23 1718 about 106 convicts are taken by lighter from Blackwall, London, to the Eagle Galley at Long Reach, bound under the 1717 Transportation Act for Virginia and Maryland; the master certifies 107 received on 28 August. Butler quotes a newspaper notice of the voyage; the names of the 107 are in the master's certificate (Calendar of Treasury Books, vol. 32). This is the operational proof-of-concept voyage for Track C: the Managerial Class demonstrates that the domestic Asiento can move human cargo at scale (100+ persons per voyage) using the identical logistics as the Middle Passage. The classification 'felon' has been fully operationalized as a property-transfer category.
sources: [A live_verified] src-butler-ahr-1896 https://www.jstor.org/stable/1833611; [A live_verified] src-w3cc-calendar-of-treasury-books-vol-32-1718-treasury-d743 https://www.british-history.ac.uk/cal-treasury-books/vol32/pp577-596

[w3-1718-forward-corporate] W3 August 1718 corporate TA
Jonathan Forward operation: Tier 5 operational contractor of Track C, domestic mirror of SSC Tier 5 role in Track A
Same event as w3-1718-forward-financial. Corporate-lane consequence: the Forward operation is the first sole-proprietor Tier 5 contractor in the three-track labor system. Unlike the SSC (Track A, joint-stock corporation) and the SC colonial government (Track B, sovereign body), Forward is a London merchant holding the Treasury contract in his own name, the sole Treasury contractor until 1739. This is the archetype of the modern private-contractor model: individual operator with monopoly access to a state-guaranteed revenue stream from a classified population. The Trustee Hierarchy Five-Tier Refinement (FW-012) is empirically demonstrated: Forward is Tier 5 executing on Track C for Tier 1 merchant-banker benefit.

[w3-1718-forward-financial] W3 August 1718 financial TA MILESTONE
Jonathan Forward Treasury contract: L3 per-head subsidy plus monopoly on Track C transport
The British Treasury awards Jonathan Forward the first Treasury contract for transporting convicts (articles of agreement, 8 August 1718) under the 1717 Transportation Act. Terms: L3 per head paid by Treasury for felons from Newgate and the Home Counties gaols, one contractor at a time. Forward becomes the Tier 5 operational contractor of Track C in exact parallel to the South Sea Company as Tier 5 operational contractor of Track A. The financial mechanism is identical: state-guaranteed per-head payment to a private contractor for delivering classified persons to a colonial market. Domestic Asiento is now operational.

[w3-1718-legal-april] W3 April 26 1718 legal TA MILESTONE
First Old Bailey transportation sentences under 4 Geo 1 c 11: 27 to 29 malefactors sentenced to transportation
The Old Bailey criminal court hands down the first sentences of transportation under the Transportation Act 1717 on April 26 1718: between 27 and 29 persons classified as 'malefactors' are sentenced to transportation to the American colonies. Operational first-fire of Track C. From this date forward the classification 'felon' converts English poor into property-transfer contracts under statutory backing. Butler's 1896 paper reports the sentences from a contemporary periodical, the Historical Register.

[w3-1718-sovereign] W3 March 1718 sovereign TA MILESTONE
Coweta Resolution: Emperor Brim locks Britain, France, and Spain into 45-year Creek neutrality
Emperor Brim of the Coweta town of the Muskogee Creek Confederacy with representatives of Britain, France, and Spain present at Coweta, joins other Creek leaders in crafting the Coweta Resolution, a Creek policy of neutrality among the three powers. The doctrine holds for 45 years until the 1763 Treaty of Paris redraws imperial claims. This is the largest single sovereign counter-move documented in W3: the Muskogee Confederacy uses tri-imperial rivalry to preserve autochthonous jurisdiction against all three colonial systems at once.

Robbery Act increases Forward operation revenue by expanding qualifying convict pool
Same event as w3-1719-legal. Financial-lane consequence: expanding the input population expands Forward's per-head revenue capture. This is the moment the Treasury per-head subsidy becomes a scalable revenue mechanism, no longer limited to the narrow 1717 categories.

[w3-1719-legal] W3 1719 legal TA
Robbery Act 6 Geo 1 c 23 expands input funnel to Track C by adding transportable offenses
The 1719 Robbery Act extends the Transportation Act 1717 by adding new statutory categories of transportable offenses, widening the input funnel to Track C. This is Phase 4 Integration of the Administrative Patch Cycle: the initial statute (1717) is now being expanded into the broader legal framework so that additional populations qualify for classification-into-transportable-status. Standard patch-cycle expansion pattern: install the mechanism first, then broaden its inputs on subsequent legislative sessions.

[w3-1720-corporate] W3 June 1720 corporate TA
Bubble Act cements SSC as sole joint-stock franchise for Track A, foreclosing competitor Track A operators
Same event as w3-1720-financial. Corporate-lane consequence: the Bubble Act bars new unchartered joint-stock companies, leaving existing chartered companies and trade in partnership untouched. The SSC holds the Asiento, the monopoly on supplying slaves to Spanish America; the British colonies' own slave trade has been open since 1698 to the Royal African Company and separate traders. This is direct parallel to Jonathan Forward's monopoly contract for Track C (1718). Both tracks are now controlled by single Tier 5 operators with statutory-guaranteed monopoly access to state-guaranteed revenue streams.

[w3-1720-financial] W3 June 1720 financial TA MILESTONE
Bubble Act 6 Geo 1 c 18 legally monopolizes joint-stock form for SSC, three months before September SSC crash
The Bubble Act receives Royal Assent in June 1720, three months before the September to December 1720 SSC crash. The Act criminalizes new unchartered joint-stock formations, shielding the SSC from competition for investors. It passed in June 1720, before the crash, and scholars read it as protecting the South Sea Company from competing schemes. This is the N1 Collapse-Survival Pattern operating as preemptive legal armor: the state installs statutory protection for the classification-backed instrument BEFORE the instrument crashes, ensuring that when the bond fails the underlying Track A classification is preserved intact. The Company's slave trade continues after the crash, and 'some of their greatest cargoes occurred after the Bubble burst'. The bond fails; the classification does not.

[w3-1721-military] W3 1721 military TA
British construct Fort King George on Altamaha River
Britain constructs Fort King George on the Altamaha River in present-day Georgia in 1721. Ivers describes it as designed to prevent the French from controlling the Altamaha River region and to guard the southern frontier against Spaniards and Yemassee. The corpus reads it also as a response to Muscogee active-neutrality under the Coweta Doctrine; that reading is the framework's, not Ivers's. The fort represents Tier 3 administrative counter-strategy: unable to negotiate land cession, install physical infrastructure to establish territorial presence anyway. It was garrisoned from 1722 by an Independent Company of invalids and old men, who endured heavy hardship from disease, threats of Spanish and Indian attack, and the coastal environment (Georgia State Parks); the company was withdrawn in 1727 to protect the Port Royal settlers.
sources: [A citation_only] src-ivers-british-drums; [B live_verified] src-gastateparks-fort-king-george https://gastateparks.org/FortKingGeorge
links: w3-1721-records, w3-1718-sovereign
threads: T-COWETA-DOCTRINE

[w3-1721-records] W3 1721 records TA
Board of Trade Representation of 1721: Indian nations between Carolina and the French, some trading with both
Martin Bladen and colleagues of the Board of Trade sign the Representation on the state of the plantations on the continent of America, 8 September 1721. Of about 9,200 Indian fighting men between Carolina and the French settlements on the Mississippi, it counts 3,400 'entirely debauched to the french interest' by the French fort at the Alabamas, about 2,000 who 'trade at present, indifferently with both', and 3,800 Cherokees still at enmity with the French. It fears a French route from Mobile down the Altamaha, and it recommends new forts. The corpus reads the 2,000 who trade with both as the Lower Creek neutrality it calls the Coweta Doctrine; the report does not name the Creeks, Coweta or Brims, and records no land-cession negotiation.
sources: [A live_verified] src-bladen-report-1721 https://archive.org/details/cu31924032193629; [A citation_only] src-metcalf-royal-government
links: w3-1718-sovereign
threads: T-COWETA-DOCTRINE

[w3-1725-financial] W3 1725 financial TA MILESTONE
Asiento-classified slave carriage peaks: N1 pattern proof
The South Sea Bubble crash of 1720 does not end the South Sea Company's slave carrying: it keeps trading under the Asiento, and 'some of their greatest cargoes occurred after the Bubble burst'. The classification code (Asiento standing under 1713 Treaty of Utrecht) has integrated into imperial statute and no longer requires SSC as executing corporation. Sub-contracting private carriers under SSC nominal license carry the majority of voyages (not yet sourced). This is the empirical foundation of Collapse-Survival Pattern N1: the corporate wrapper is replaceable, the classification franchise is not. The Company's carriage peaks in the mid-1720s, until 'When Britain clashed again with Spain in 1726, the company's trade apparently came to a halt' (Paul, from the Voyages Database).

[w3-1725-population] W3 1725 population TA
Peak trafficked volumes under decentralized Asiento operation
Population-processing throughput reaches Asiento-license peak in the mid-1720s. Volumes carried on the Company's own voyages after 1720 exceed those of 1713 to 1720. The classification (Asiento standing) remains valid; the operational structure has decentralized. Population-processing continues after the crash, though its operations are suspended during the Anglo-Spanish wars of 1718-1723 and 1727-1729, and no ships sail in 1731.

[w3-1732-land] W3 1732 land TA
Yamacraw Bluff settlement via heir-broker workaround
Mary Musgrove's brokered negotiation with Tomochichi and the Yamacraw enables Oglethorpe's 1733 Savannah settlement without direct English-Muscogee land-cession negotiation. The workaround preserves Coweta-Doctrine sovereignty at the Muscogee-Confederacy level (no formal cession by the Confederacy) while enabling English settlement at the local-band level (Yamacraw grant to specific parties). This is heir-broker jurisdictional workaround: sovereignty-preserving at the framework level while jurisdiction-permitting at the operational level.

[w3-1732-legal] W3 1732 legal TA MILESTONE
Georgia Charter granted; Musgrove positioned as Creek-English broker
George II grants the Georgia Charter to James Oglethorpe and trustees on June 9, 1732. The charter authorizes settlement between the Savannah and Altamaha Rivers, land under Muscogee sovereignty per Coweta Doctrine. The colonial project sidesteps the doctrinal problem by using Mary Musgrove (Coosaponakeesa), a Creek-English woman with sovereign-family standing on the Muscogee side and English trader connections, who from February 1733 serves as Oglethorpe's principal interpreter with the Yamacraw chief Tomochichi at Yamacraw Bluff; the corpus reads her as the diplomatic broker. Musgrove's dual standing enables land-use negotiations that direct English negotiators could not conduct under the Coweta framework.

[w3-1738-counter-move] W3 1738 counter-move TA
Jurisdictional-alternative counter-strategy: exploit imperial rivalry to void chattel classification
The Fort Mose framework demonstrates the Coweta-Doctrine strategic logic operating in a different context: exploit multi-imperial rivalry to obtain sovereign standing that voids the master classification. The escaped enslaved persons remain the same persons before and after crossing the border; only the applicable jurisdiction changes. Spain's grant of freedom is conditioned on conversion to Catholicism and serves as a weapon against the English slave-plantation economy; the corpus reads the strategic side as the operative one. The English attack on Fort Mose in June 1740, during the War of Jenkins' Ear, shows the settlement was militarily significant enough to be attacked.
sources: [A citation_only] src-landers-black-society-florida
links: w3-1738-legal, w3-1750-counter-move

[w3-1738-land] W3 1738 land TA
Physical settlement established two miles north of St Augustine
Fort Mose is constructed as a fortified free-Black settlement two miles north of St Augustine, Florida. Approximately 100 residents initially, until Oglethorpe's 1740 invasion overran it; it was not resettled until 1752. Physically instantiates the sanctuary policy on Spanish-controlled land close to the English colonies (Georgia, founded 1733, lay between Florida and South Carolina). The physical proximity to English territory is the operational choice: its militia guarded St. Augustine's northern approach, and the freedom policy drew enslaved people away from the English colonies; the corpus reads the provocation of English retaliation as intended.

[w3-1738-legal] W3 1738 legal TA MILESTONE
Spanish royal cedula grants sanctuary status to escaped enslaved persons
Florida's governor, Manuel de Montiano, acting on the crown's 1693 proclamation, grants the runaways freedom and establishes Gracia Real de Santa Teresa de Mose at St Augustine, Florida. Escaped enslaved persons who reach Spanish territory and convert to Catholicism receive legal freedom under Spanish jurisdiction. The legal instrument voids English chattel-property claims through jurisdictional-alternative, not through direct challenge. This is Coweta-Doctrine-adjacent counter-strategy operating in an imperial-rivalry context.

[w3-1739-financial] W3 1739 financial TA
Jonathan Forward monopoly ends; Andrew Reid succeeds as Track C Tier 5 contractor
The Transportation Act 1717/18 convict-shipping monopoly held by Jonathan Forward ends in 1739. Andrew Reid succeeds as government-contracted convict shipper. The transition demonstrates FW-014 Collapse-Survival Pattern N1 operating on Track C of the Three-Track Labor System: the corporate wrapper (Forward's firm) is replaced by successor (Reid's firm), but the classification franchise (Transportation Act legal category of 'transportable felon' under 7 or 14 year terms) persists unchanged. The Track C operation continues without interruption; the operator changes.

[w3-1739-legal] W3 1739 legal TA
Classification franchise persists across operator succession
The 1717/18 Transportation Act statutory framework is unchanged by the Forward-Reid succession. The 'transportable felon' legal category, the 7 and 14 year term structure, and the sovereign-monopoly contracting arrangement all persist. The succession is an operational-manager change at Tier 5, not a legal-framework change at Tier 1. Empirical proof, at the smallest scale, of the Collapse-Survival Pattern N1 template that later executes at systemic scale in the SC 1740 Negro Act, 1865 Thirteenth Amendment convict-lease exception, and 1924 Immigration Act.
sources: [A citation_only] src-ekirch-bound-for-america
links: w3-1739-financial
threads: T-VAGRANCY-CHAIN, T-THREE-TRACK-LABOR

[w3-1740-legal] W3 1740 legal TA MILESTONE
SC Negro Act codifies 58-section comprehensive slave code
South Carolina General Assembly enacts Act No. 670, comprising 58 sections of comprehensive slave code addressing movement restrictions, gathering limits, literacy prohibition, arms prohibition, and slaveholder authority. Passed as direct legislative response to the Stono Rebellion of September 1739. Establishes 'Negro' as a comprehensive legal classification, not merely a demographic descriptor. Template statute that the 1790 US Naturalization Act and 1924 Racial Integrity Act inherit and refine. Framework significance: this is the classification statute that the 1790 Sundry Free Moors petition tests as counter-move in Window 4.

[w3-1740-population] W3 1740 population TA
Comprehensive population-processing regime: movement, literacy, gathering
Beyond the classification itself, the 1740 Act installs the operational infrastructure for population-processing: pass system for movement, statutory prohibition on literacy, gathering-size caps, arms prohibition, and slaveholder policing authority. Movement-control provisions echo the English 1662 Settlement Act mechanically. Literacy prohibition prevents access to legal remedy. Gathering caps prevent coordinated resistance following Stono. This is the SC template that Georgia adopts virtually unchanged in 1755.

[w3-1750-counter-move] W3 1750 counter-move TA
Sovereign admission against interest: the 73-year documentary firewall
Britain's acceptance of the Sultan's style as ruler of 'all the West of Africa', which the corpus reads as an acknowledgment of Moroccan sovereignty over West African territory, operates as counter-move to the Discovery-Doctrine architecture that Britain and successor US jurisdictions rely on for land-title claims. The counter-move mechanism: no future US Discovery-Doctrine claim can be sustained without addressing that Britain (the sovereign source from which US inherits Discovery-Doctrine authority via colonial charter chain) formally acknowledged the exact opposite in the 1750 treaty. The counter-move is documentary and jurisdictional, not military.

[w3-1750-financial] W3 1750 financial TA
Treaty of Madrid: Britain relinquishes Asiento to Spain
The Treaty of Madrid (October 5, 1750) between Britain and Spain ends Britain's Asiento, inherited from the 1713 Treaty of Utrecht, Britain giving up the four remaining years granted at Aix-la-Chapelle (1748). Spain agrees to pay 100,000 pounds sterling to the Royal Assiento Company. Read under FW-014 Collapse-Survival Pattern N1: the corporate wrapper (Asiento license) is relinquished, but the classification franchise (chattel-standing under 'Negro' classification) survives and consolidates into private-carrier arrangements outside SSC's formal license structure. The British Asiento ends; Spain later grants a new asiento to a Spanish company (1765 to 1779). The corpus reads the classification as surviving the wrapper.

[w3-1750-legal] W3 1750 legal TA MILESTONE
Britain formally recognizes Moroccan sovereignty over West African littoral
Treaty of Peace and Commerce between Great Britain and Morocco 1750 is concluded with the Emperor under his full style, 'King and Emperor of Fez, Mequinez, Morocco, and all the West of Africa'; the corpus reads Britain's acceptance of that style as a sovereign admission against interest, made 72 years before Johnson v M'Intosh (1823) (the treaty is dated January 1750 Old Style, January 1751 in modern dating) attempts to install the Discovery Doctrine as US federal law. The treaty text preserves the pre-1823 documentary record that the sovereignty framework of the American Levant contains a bilateral treaty acknowledgment that the Discovery Doctrine premise is factually false at the West African end of the trans-Atlantic trade chain.

[w3-1751-counter-move] W3 1751 counter-move TA
Anglo-Moroccan consular relations reinforced: two sovereign admissions in 13 months
Following the 1750 Anglo-Moroccan Treaty, Britain, which already had a consul at Tetuan (recorded in 1729) and negotiated the treaty through its Consul General William Petticrew, keeps consular representation in 1751, operationalizing the treaty's sovereign-recognition premise. The consular framework requires ongoing British acknowledgment of Moroccan sovereign authority as a functioning imperial power, not merely a treaty counterparty. Combined with the 1750 treaty, the treaty (15 January 1750 Old Style, January 1751 in modern dating) and its additional articles, concluded at Fez days apart and ratified together on 31 July 1751, produce two documented British sovereign admissions in the same year, both surviving into the archival record as counter-authority available for Software-Attack modality invocation against Discovery-Doctrine sovereignty claims.

[w3-1751-information] W3 1751 information TA
Publication as instrument of demographic-policy legitimation
The 1755 Boston publication of Observations Concerning the Increase of Mankind converts Franklin's private manuscript into a public policy artifact. Reprinted in London, Edinburgh, Philadelphia, and elsewhere through the 1760s. Quoted approvingly by Malthus from the second edition of his Essay on the Principle of Population (1803); the 1798 first edition does not name Franklin. The corpus reads the information-architecture significance as the essay becoming the citable authority for subsequent whitening-project legislation, converting Franklin from private commentator to legitimating source.

Consular operationalization of 1750 treaty framework
The 1751 consular arrangements formalize ongoing diplomatic recognition. British consular officers at Tetuan and Sale operate under Moroccan sovereign license, receiving credentials from the Emperor's court. The legal effect is continuous recognition rather than one-time treaty acknowledgment; each renewed credential is a fresh sovereign admission against interest.

[w3-1751-population] W3 1751 population TA MILESTONE
Franklin names the Whitening Project explicitly
Benjamin Franklin drafts Observations Concerning the Increase of Mankind in 1751. The essay articulates the demographic engineering thesis: colonial North America should exclude 'all Blacks and Tawneys' in order to increase 'the lovely White and Red.' Describes America (exclusive of the new comers) as 'wholly' tawny relative to a small English contingent. This is an explicit statement of the Whitening Project as demographic policy by a leading colonist, after earlier colonial laws (South Carolina, 1698 and 1716) that aimed to raise the white share of the population. Written in 1751, the essay circulates in manuscript from spring 1752 and is first published in Boston in 1755. Sets the demographic-engineering framing that the 1790 Naturalization Act codifies into US federal law.
sources: [A live_verified] src-franklin-observations-1751 https://founders.archives.gov/documents/Franklin/01-04-02-0080; [A citation_only] src-wood-franklin; [A live_verified] src-w3cc-the-statutes-at-large-of-south-carolina-vol-2-co-f519 https://archive.org/download/statutesatlargeo02edit/statutesatlargeo02edit_djvu.txt

[w3-1755-records] W3 1755 records TA
Lisbon Earthquake: Lisbon archives and libraries lost, the Torre do Tombo rescued
The Lisbon Earthquake and subsequent fire and tsunami on November 1, 1755, destroy the Casa da India and the Casa dos Contos. Kendrick records the Braganza archive burnt and great private libraries lost, and the archives of the damaged Torre do Tombo rescued: the recovery of royal and official archives 'was given a high priority'. Records-destruction effect, as the corpus reads it: documentation held in Lisbon on the Portuguese Atlantic was reduced, so later accounts lean more on what survived elsewhere. The Overseas Council's own archive survived: the fire made some of its books disappear, some later reconstituted by copy, and the archive was reportedly moved after the earthquake (Martins 2018). The cited sources do not establish which other Atlantic series were lost.
sources: [A citation_only] src-kendrick-lisbon-earthquake; [A citation_only] src-chase-1755-lisbon; [A live_verified] src-martins-conselho-ultramarino-2018 https://www.redalyc.org/journal/263/26358185003/html/

[w3-1758-information] W3 1758 information TA MILESTONE
Linnaeus Systema Naturae 10th edition installs four-race scientific taxonomy
Carl Linnaeus publishes the 10th edition of Systema Naturae in 1758. The four geographic varieties of Homo sapiens had stood in every edition since 1735; the 10th edition adds to each a temperament and behavioural and governmental descriptors: europaeus (white, sanguine), americanus (red, choleric), asiaticus (yellow, melancholic), afer (black, phlegmatic), and two more varieties, Ferus and Monstrosus. This is the taxonomic overlay that converts Franklin's 1751 demographic-engineering premise into scientific authority. The classifications carry temperamental and behavioral attributions attached to physical descriptors, installing the 'race as inherent essence' premise into the leading scientific framework of the 18th century. Downstream: Blumenbach 1795 five-race taxonomy inherits and refines; the 1924 immigration quotas rest on the eugenic race science of Madison Grant and others, which still borrows Linnaean terms; the corpus reads 19th c anthropometry and modern population genetics as inheriting the same reference-population architecture.

[w3-1758-population] W3 1758 population TA
Taxonomic codification converts demographic policy into scientific classification
Linnaeus's four-race taxonomy operates as scientific-authority legitimation of the demographic-policy framing Franklin's 1751 essay had articulated. Once populations are classified taxonomically, statute-level classification (SC Negro Act 1740, 1790 Naturalization Act) can invoke scientific taxonomy as authoritative basis. The classification code (V2.1 §II.4 Software Neutrality) obtains a scientific-authority overlay that survives subsequent challenges by relocating the classification from political to biological ground.

[w3-1763-land] W3 1763 land TA MILESTONE
Continental cession consolidates British sovereignty east of Mississippi
Physical territorial consequence of the 1763 treaty: British sovereignty extends from Hudson Bay to Florida, and from the Atlantic to the Mississippi. The autochthonous polities inhabiting this territory (Muscogee, Cherokee, Iroquois Confederacy, and dozens of smaller nations) receive no representation in the treaty and no cession by their own act. The territorial reassignment is between European sovereigns; the inhabitants of the ceded territory are treated as fixture rather than principal. The 1763 Royal Proclamation attempts to manage the tension by drawing a boundary at the Appalachians, but the classification of autochthonous polities as subjects of British sovereignty is now the legal frame.

[w3-1763-legal] W3 1763 legal TA MILESTONE
Treaty of Paris ends Seven Years War; France cedes North American holdings east of Mississippi to Britain
The 1763 Treaty of Paris terminates the Seven Years War (1756-1763). France cedes Canada and all its territory east of the Mississippi River, except New Orleans, to Britain; Spain cedes Florida to Britain in exchange for Havana. The treaty eliminates France as an imperial counterweight to British power in North America. This is the Phase-0 Trigger event for Window 3's endgame: it terminates the multi-European-power context that made Coweta-Doctrine active neutrality operationally coherent, and it forces autochthonous polities across the eastern woodlands into a bilateral rather than multilateral diplomatic posture. Immediate downstream: 1763 Royal Proclamation, Pontiac's War 1763-1766, the political conditions for 1776 breakaway.

[w3-1765-counter-move] W3 1765 counter-move TB
Military validation of the 1750 sovereign-admission-against-interest
The Larache engagement supplies the empirical foundation missing from the 1750 treaty's paper acknowledgment: Morocco can defend the sovereignty Britain acknowledged. Under international law of the period, sovereignty claims require capacity to defend the territory claimed; the Larache defense operationalizes that requirement. Counter-move mechanism: the 1750 treaty plus the 1765 defense together produce a complete sovereignty claim (paper plus arms) that any subsequent US Discovery-Doctrine invocation must address to be sustained.

[w3-1765-military] W3 1765 military TB
French naval expedition against Larache repulsed by Moroccan forces
Sultan Mohammed III (Sidi Mohammed ben Abdellah) directs Moroccan naval and coastal defense forces to repel a French expeditionary force at Larache on the Atlantic coast. The engagement demonstrates that Morocco retains empirical military capacity to defend its sovereign territorial integrity against a European great power, three years before the more famous 1769 Anglo-Moroccan engagement at Al-Hoceima. Empirical proof that the 1750 British sovereign admission is not merely diplomatic recognition of a decayed power but recognition of an operationally functioning sovereign state.

[w3-1769-counter-move] W3 1769 counter-move TA
Territorial reversal of Discovery-Doctrine occupation
Mazagan expulsion operates as counter-move against the earliest Discovery-Doctrine settlement pattern. Portugal's claim to Mazagan rested on the 1455 Romanus Pontifex Papal Bull and the 1494 Treaty of Tordesillas. Reversing that occupation via military action, not treaty renegotiation, demonstrates the counter-strategy modality that neither the 1750 Anglo-Moroccan Treaty (paper) nor the 1765 Larache defense (defense) had proven: the sovereign can reclaim territory previously classified as Discovery-Doctrine possession. This is the 66-year Counter-Offensive's territorial-reversal round.

[w3-1769-military] W3 1769 military TA MILESTONE
Sultan Mohammed III expels Portuguese from Mazagan
Sultan Sidi Mohammed ben Abdellah completes the siege and expulsion of the Portuguese garrison from Mazagan (El Jadida) on the Atlantic coast. Portugal had held Mazagan continuously since 1502, 267 years. The expulsion terminates the last Portuguese enclave on the Moroccan Atlantic and closes a colonial-occupation lane running back to the earliest Discovery-Doctrine Papal Bulls. Empirical proof, four years after Larache 1765, that Moroccan military capacity is sufficient not only to defend but to reverse European territorial encroachment on Moroccan sovereign coast.

[w3-1772-counter-move] W3 1772 counter-move TA
Metropole-vs-colony jurisdictional division as counter-strategy
Somerset produces a jurisdictional-alternative counter-strategy distinct from the Coweta and Fort Mose precedents. Coweta 1718 used multi-European-power neutrality; Fort Mose 1738 used inter-imperial rivalry (English vs Spanish). Somerset uses intra-imperial jurisdictional division (English metropole vs colonial jurisdiction). All three counter-strategies operate on the same principle: the person does not change, the applicable jurisdiction does, and the classification that governs the person changes with jurisdiction. Empirical proof that the classification code (V2.1 §II.4 Software Neutrality) is jurisdiction-dependent, not person-dependent.
sources: [A citation_only] src-wise-though-heavens; [A citation_only] src-van-cleve-slaveholders
links: w3-1772-legal, w3-1738-counter-move
threads: T-DISCOVERY-DOCTRINE

[w3-1772-legal] W3 1772 legal TA MILESTONE
Somerset v Stewart: Mansfield ruling voids chattel classification on English soil
Court of King's Bench, Lord Mansfield presiding, rules that James Somerset cannot be forcibly removed from England to be sold in Jamaica. Mansfield's ruling: no positive law of England authorizes slavery; therefore Somerset cannot be held as chattel while in English jurisdiction. The ruling does not abolish English colonial slavery, but it produces a jurisdictional-alternative counter-authority: the same person classified as chattel in Virginia is a free person in England. This is jurisdictional-alternative counter-strategy operating within a single imperial system, not through inter-imperial rivalry (Fort Mose 1738 precedent) but through metropole-vs-colony legal division.

[w3-1776-legal] W3 1776 legal TA
Break-charter operation: colonial charters voided; new sovereign entity claimed
The Declaration operates as break-charter instrument: it asserts termination of the colonial charter chain running back to the Papal Bull / English Crown source and installs a new sovereign entity claiming direct standing. Under the Discovery Doctrine framework, this transfer is impossible without Crown consent; the Declaration proceeds without such consent, creating a legal fiction that the 1783 Treaty of Paris later formalizes. Framework significance: the break-charter does not address the Discovery-Doctrine premise itself, which the new sovereign entity inherits and later formalizes into federal law via Johnson v M'Intosh 1823.

[w3-1776-sovereign] W3 1776 sovereign TA MILESTONE
Continental Congress declares independence from Great Britain
The Continental Congress adopts the Declaration of Independence on July 4, 1776. Read through the Trustee Hierarchy framework, this is a Tier 4 (Colonial Elite) breakaway from Tier 3 (Imperial Coordinator, Board of Trade / Privy Council) that preserves rather than terminates the underlying Tier 1 (merchant-banker) Trustee position. The Navigation-Act tariff revenue captured by British merchant-bankers pre-1776 does not vanish; it reroutes through new Tier 5 operational structures under the same Tier 1 ownership. The breakaway is administrative reorganization, not sovereign discharge. The Chesapeake Class Split (FW-011) framework applies: the same Tier 3 vs Tier 4 optimization conflict that produced the 1718 Transportation Act dispute produces the 1776 breakaway six decades later.

[w3-1777-certification] W3 1777 certification TA MILESTONE
Sultan Mohammed III issues first sovereign recognition of United States
On December 20, 1777, Sultan Sidi Mohammed ben Abdellah issues a declaration opening Moroccan ports to American shipping and de facto recognizing the United States as a sovereign entity. This is the first sovereign recognition of US independence by any foreign power. The recognition is unsolicited: no US diplomatic mission had approached the Moroccan court. Framework significance: the first foreign state to recognize American sovereignty is not a European power but the Moroccan sultanate, the same sovereign whose 1750 Anglo-Moroccan Treaty produced the 73-year documentary firewall against the Discovery Doctrine. Diplomatic precondition for the 1786-87 Treaty of Peace and Friendship.

[w3-1777-counter-move] W3 1777 counter-move TA
Sovereign-standing preservation across the 1776 break-charter
Moroccan sovereign recognition transfers the sovereign-standing counter-authority framework across the 1776 break-charter transition. The 1750 Anglo-Moroccan Treaty acknowledged Moroccan sovereignty against British interest; the 1777 Sultan recognition establishes bilateral standing directly with the new US entity, bypassing any need for British inheritance chain. Result: the counter-authority framework survives the break-charter intact and later inherits directly into US federal law via the 1786-87 treaty and Article VI ratification 1787.
sources: [A citation_only] src-parker-uncle-sam-barbary
links: w3-1777-certification, w3-1750-counter-move
threads: T-DISCOVERY-DOCTRINE

[w3-1778-financial] W3 1778 financial TA MILESTONE
Treaty of Alliance installs Bourbon Mortgage: France as Tier 2 Senior Lienholder
The Franco-American Treaty of Alliance (February 6, 1778) and its companion Treaty of Amity and Commerce commit France to military support of US independence if war breaks out between France and Britain; French money comes separately, in loans and gifts set out in the Franklin-Vergennes contracts of 1782 and 1783. Read as debt instrument under V2.1 §III.1, this is the installation moment of the Bourbon Mortgage: France (Bourbon dynasty) becomes the Tier 2 Senior Lienholder of the American Levant. Those contracts come to about 43 million livres (18 million lent, a 10 million Dutch loan under French guarantee, 6 million more in 1783, and 9 million given), repayable in specie in fixed instalments under the general guaranty of the thirteen states. The loans are settled in 1795 and the alliance set aside by the Convention of 1800; the corpus reads the position as passing on through later franchise reassignments, including a 1815 Rothschild transfer (not yet sourced).
sources: [A live_verified] src-treaty-of-alliance-1778 https://avalon.law.yale.edu/18th_century/fr1788-1.asp; [A citation_only] src-bemis-diplomacy-revolution; [A citation_only] src-dull-french-navy; [A live_verified] src-treaty-alliance-1778-text https://avalon.law.yale.edu/18th_century/fr1788-2.asp; [A live_verified] src-w3cc-contract-between-the-king-and-the-thirteen-unite-dc88 https://avalon.law.yale.edu/18th_century/fr-1782.asp; [A live_verified] src-w3cc-contract-between-the-king-and-the-thirteen-unite-c774 https://avalon.law.yale.edu/18th_century/fr-1783.asp; [B live_verified] src-w3cc-office-of-the-historian-u-s-debt-and-foreign-loa-7f8f https://history.state.gov/milestones/1784-1800/loans
links: w3-1776-sovereign, w3-1787-legal
threads: T-FRANCHISE-TRANSFER, T-MERCHANT-BANKER-CONSTANT, T-TRUSTEE-HIERARCHY-FIVE-TIER

[w3-1778-legal] W3 1778 legal TA
Treaty commits future US revenue streams as debt collateral
Treaty of Amity and Commerce Articles II to V grant most-favored-nation treatment both ways: Article III to French subjects in US ports, Article IV to Americans in French ports, and Article V exempts US ships from France's 100-sols-per-ton duty. The corpus reads the treaties, combined with the Alliance Treaty's military commitment, as an implicit debt-collateral structure extending French support against future US revenue-stream access; neither 1778 treaty contains money. The 1778 treaties outlive the 1783 peace and cause friction in the 1790s; Congress abrogates them in 1798 and the Convention of 1800 sets them aside.

[w3-1778-sovereign] W3 1778 sovereign TA
Second sovereign recognition (France) formalizes new-nation standing
France's two treaties of 6 February 1778 constitute its formal sovereign recognition of the US, following Morocco's declaration of 20 December 1777 opening its ports to American ships, a de facto recognition (Morocco's formal recognition comes with the 1786 treaty). Two sovereign recognitions within seven weeks (Morocco December 1777, France February 1778) establish the new US entity's standing in international law before the 1783 Treaty of Paris terminates British contest. The pair of recognitions represents different Trustee tiers: Morocco recognizing outside the European Trustee hierarchy; France recognizing as Tier 2 Senior Lienholder installing the Bourbon Mortgage.

[w3-1781-financial] W3 1781 financial TB
Zong: an insurance claim on people thrown overboard
November 1781. Approximately 132 enslaved people were thrown from the ship Zong, and the owners subsequently claimed on the insurance policy covering them as cargo. The resulting litigation, Gregson v. Gilbert, turned on whether the loss was recoverable under the policy. This entry exists because construct-insurance-actuarial-sublane names it as a member and the W6 dossier on credit default swaps references it as the origin of pricing risk on lives the insurer does not hold; neither could resolve it.
sources: [B live_verified] src-insurance-museum-zong-1781 https://insurance.museum/history-article-the-zong-massacre-1781-an-insurers-perspective

[w3-1781-records] W3 1781 records TB
The case record survives as an insurance dispute
What survives in the law reports is a commercial argument about recoverability under a marine policy. The people are present in the record as insured cargo, which is the documentary form the corpus tracks: the classification is not asserted in the case, it is presupposed by the pleadings.
sources: [B live_verified] src-insurance-museum-zong-1781 https://insurance.museum/history-article-the-zong-massacre-1781-an-insurers-perspective

[w3-1783-land] W3 1783 land TA
Territorial transfer without autochthonous representation
The 1783 treaty transfers sovereign standing over territory from Great Lakes to 31st parallel, from Atlantic to Mississippi. Autochthonous polities inhabiting the ceded territory (Iroquois Confederacy, Ohio Valley confederacies, Muscogee, Cherokee) receive no representation and no consent-to-cede. The territorial reassignment operates entirely between European-derived sovereigns. Downstream: the Northwest Indian War (usually dated 1786-1795) and 1794 Battle of Fallen Timbers demonstrate that autochthonous polities do not accept the treaty's terms; military enforcement is required to convert paper transfer into operational reality.

[w3-1783-legal] W3 1783 legal TA
Treaty of Paris ends Revolutionary War; Britain recognizes US independence
The Treaty of Paris (September 3, 1783) formally ends the American Revolutionary War. Britain recognizes US sovereignty over territory east of the Mississippi from the Great Lakes to the 31st parallel. The recognition formalizes what the Sultan's 1777 declaration opening Moroccan ports and the 1778 French recognition had established internationally, but explicitly terminates British sovereign contest and rewires the Discovery-Doctrine inheritance chain: the US now inherits Discovery-Doctrine claims directly from British Crown transfer rather than via colonial-charter derivation. The 1783 peace does not cancel the 1778 obligations to France; the war loans are settled in 1795 and the alliance ends with the Convention of 1800.

[w3-1784-counter-move] W3 1784 counter-move TA
Naval leverage as counter-strategy against unarmed successor state
The Betsey seizure operates as counter-move on the same jurisdictional-alternative logic as the 1750 treaty and 1777 recognition, but adds the military-leverage dimension. Adams and Jefferson (Adams in Paris, then London from mid-1785; Jefferson in Paris) treat the seizure as forcing function requiring diplomatic settlement rather than military response, which the US cannot mount. Result: the sequence from 1784 seizure to 1786-87 treaty ratification places Morocco in the leverage position as Congress ratifies the treaty on 18 July 1787, while the Federal Convention is still sitting.

[w3-1784-military] W3 1784 military TA
Moroccan naval action: brig Betsey seized off Cadiz
Moroccan corsairs operating under Sultan Mohammed III's authority seize the American merchant brig Betsey off the coast of Cadiz on October 11, 1784. Read under the 66-year Counter-Offensive frame, this is the maritime-power demonstration that follows the 1769 Mazagan territorial-reversal and 1765 Larache defense proofs. The US, one year after the 1783 Peace of Paris, has no navy and no Mediterranean protection. The seizure operationalizes the Sultan's leverage in the diplomatic sequence culminating in the 1786-87 Treaty of Peace and Friendship.

[w3-1787-certification] W3 1787 certification TA MILESTONE
Pre-Constitutional treaty firewall installed
Ratification 11 months before the ninth state ratified the Constitution (21 June 1788) and about 19 months before it took effect (4 March 1789) carries the Moroccan treaty, already ratified as a treaty by the Confederation Congress, into the 'Treaties made ... under the Authority of the United States' that Article VI makes the supreme Law of the Land. This is jurisdictional pre-installation: the firewall exists in federal law from the instant federal law exists. Any subsequent US federal-law act inconsistent with the treaty operates under the Article VI Supremacy Clause conflict framework, not de novo. Framework significance: The 1786 treaty ran for a fixed fifty years and was renewed almost word for word in 1836 by the Treaty of Meknes, which stays in force until either party gives twelve months' notice; the State Department lists the 1836 treaty as in force today and calls this 'the United States' oldest unbroken treaty'. The corpus reads it as the single most durable jurisdictional counter-authority in the audit period.

[w3-1787-counter-move] W3 1787 counter-move TA MILESTONE
The A00 lineage jurisdictional-defense modality's federal-law anchor
The 1787 treaty is the federal-law-anchored form of the V2.1 §III.5 Jurisdictional Defense modality. Under V2.1, the A00 lineage counter-strategy has three modalities: jurisdictional defense (1787 treaty firewall), memory defense (Melungeon-Nanticoke-Moor community preservation), and Software attack (yet to be operationalized). The 1787 treaty ratification installs the jurisdictional defense modality directly into US federal law, unenforced but not repealed. Its persistence into 2026, through the 1836 renewal that the State Department still lists as in force, is the strongest anchor for Software-Attack modality invocation against Johnson v M'Intosh 1823 and any subsequent Discovery-Doctrine dependent US federal claim.

[w3-1787-legal] W3 1787 legal TA MILESTONE
Treaty of Peace and Friendship, Morocco ratified July 18, 1787
The Confederation Congress ratifies the Treaty of Peace and Friendship with Morocco on July 18, 1787, negotiated by Thomas Barclay under Adams-Jefferson direction. The treaty is ratified before the US Constitution enters force (Constitution ratified June 21, 1788), which means the Moroccan treaty attaches to the not-yet-formed federal legal system through the Article VI Supremacy Clause pre-installation: any subsequent constitutional treaty framework must acknowledge the pre-existing Moroccan treaty as inherited obligation. This is the 1787 Treaty firewall: Moroccan sovereignty standing under the treaty is baked into US federal law before the Constitution establishes federal treaty-making authority.
